1.11. GLOSSARY
Segregated area.
Area delimited by physical and/or immaterial barriers with controls (interlocking devices, photocells, etc.) for
the production processes.
The controls interrupt and/or deactivate machine/system operation.
This area may not be accessed by the operator during operation but only when the machine/system is off and
in a safe state.
Working area.
Area delimited with material barriers (yellow lines and chains) for processing and/or production. This area may
not be accessed by the operator during processing but only when the machine/system is off and in a safe
state. The operator can access with standstill machine under safe condition.
Operating area of the anthropomorphic robot.
Area, bounded by physical and / or immaterial barriers with controls (laser scanner and photocells), used for
production processes.
The controls stop and / or prevent the operation of the anthropomorphic robot.
Area where access to the operator is forbidden, which can be accessed when the anthropomorphic robot is
stationary and secured.
Operator area.
Area delimited with yellow lines and overseen only by the operator performing the production processes.
Product development area.
Area in which the product in process must stay during the working phases.
Safety device.
Electrical, mechanical or hydraulic system device that prevents injury and/ or damage to persons and things;
its activation can be voluntary by the operator or automatic, induced by the presence of a hazard (the opening
of a guard, the access to a certain area).
Interlock device.
Safety micro-switch which controls the status of a protection device or a door, causing the immediate stop or
preventing the machine start, if the protection device has been removed or the door is open.
English
15/403

--- page 18 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
Final installation layout.
The installation layout is the machine/plant location plan indicating the overall dimensions and the “safety
plan” carried out (segregated area, working area, etc.).
The final installation layout is that counter-signed by the persons in charge upon commercial agreement or in
installation phase.
Always refer to the final layout to have a correct view of the machine/plant positioning and of the “safety plan”
carried out.
The final installation lay-out is inside the diagrams manual.
Installation data sheet.
The installation data sheet is a collection of technical information required to install the machine/plant
correctly so that the user can prepare everything necessary before the machine/plant is delivered.
The only official installation data sheet is the one countersigned during the sales agreement by the relative
people in charge.
Ordinary maintenance.
These are the normal machine/system control and maintenance operations that require particular technical
knowledge in order to be performed.
These operations may be carried out by the machine/plant operator in accordance with the instructions given
in this manual.
Non-routine maintenance.
These are the operations caused by unforeseeable breakage or wear, depending on particular events during
use.
The extraordinary maintenance operations must be carried out by an operator specialized by the
Manufacturer.
Packing list.
List of items that make up the machine/plant to be lifted and transported.
Refer to the list for information on the quantity, dimensions and weights of the packages.
Exposed person.
Anyone who is entirely or partly inside a danger zone.
Protection.
Part of a product used specifically to provide protection by means of a physical barrier.
Risk.
Combination of the probability and the seriousness of an injury or harm to health that may arise in a
dangerous situation.
Dangerous area.
Any area inside and/or in proximity to a machine in which the presence of an individual constitutes a personal
safety and health hazard for that particular individual.
Operator.
The operator is the person or persons, responsible for loading the raw material, for operations relating to the
process or the operation cycles, for process check, for unloading of the finished product.
English
16/403

--- page 19 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
Maintenance operator.
The operator maintenance is the person or the people in charge of transport, installation, equipment,
mechanical and electric adjustments, cleaning, routine maintenance, troubleshooting, spare parts replacement
and disassembly.
Machine off.
The machine / plant is in "off-state" mode when it has been isolated from all energy sources with its locked
disconnectors to prevent unexpected restart and residual energies have been dissipated or appropriately
reset.
Machine in momentary stop.
The machine / plant is in a "momentary stop" state when the energy sources are available and the controls are
disabled for operation.
Machine in operation.
The machine / plant is in "working machine" mode when power sources are available and controls are enabled
for operation.
Machine waiting.
The machine is in the "machine waiting" mode when the energy sources are available and the controls are
enabled for operation but an authorization must be given to reset the work cycle.
Safety guard.
Physical barrier, designed as part of the machine to provide protection.
Depending on its design, a safety guard can be called, for example, enclosure, screen, cover, curtain, door, full
segregation guard, carter, protection, mesh, fence, door.
Fixed guard.
Safety guard affixed in such a manner (for example, by screws, nuts, welding) that it can only be opened or
removed by the use of tools or by destruction of the affixing means.
Movable protection.
Safety guard which can be opened without tools.

STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
2.
DESCRIPTION OF MACHINE AND ACCESSORIES
2.1. DESCRIPTION AND BASIC FEATURES OF THE MACHINE STRETCH DRIVE 6
-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
The STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR is designed and built, when used in the conditions
provided by the manufacturer, for the production of metal wire through the stretching and drawing
process.
|
Note
Wire drawing process:
The processing of the rebar occurs through a cold rolling process.
GRUPPO
2.1.1. LIST OF MACHINE SET-UPS
The machine can be configured in the following set-ups:
‒
vertical pay-off system:
Wire rod double forwarding device.
‒
Wire feeding unit.
‒
Wire insertion radio remote control.
2.1.2. LIST OF MACHINE CONFIGURATIONS

‒
Rolling cassette PROMILLS PRO 12331.
‒
Refrigerator.
English
25/403

--- page 28 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
2.1.3. INFORMATION ABOUT THE INSPECTION SOFTWARE
The automatic functions, the programming and the machine/plant operative sequence are run by a computer
numerical control CNC.
The machine/plant shows a continuous monitoring of the work conditions and of the process parameters
through suitable devices.
The interface man machine/plant is simple and intuitive.
The main functions of the machine/plant are on a monitor of the control console.
|
Note
Read the machine inspection software user's manual carefully.
2.1.4. SHAPE OF THE RAW MATERIAL
The raw material has to be in coils with specific dimensions, weight and diameter of the rebar.
2.1.5. DEFINITION OF THE FUNCTIONAL AREAS
The machine/plant is divided into 5 functional areas.
A -
Rebar unwinding area.
The rebar is unwound ensuring the machine supply.
B -
Rebar processing area.
B1 - Material surface treatment area.
The rebar is lubricated.
B2 - Rolling area.
The rebar is rolled.
C -
Straightening area and rebar cut.
The rebar is straightened and cut to the requested size.
GRUPPO
2.1.6. INFORMATION
ABOUT THE EXTERNAL
PRODUCTIVE UNIT (UPE)
The machine has to be
connected, depending on the
production process, to
another machine called
"external productive
unit"(UPE).
GRUPPO

2.1.7. COMPOSITION OF THE MACHINE
Here below is the composition of the machine/plant in its main components.
GRUPPO
BRANO
A -
The machine has a unit called vertical pay-off system SV-7 PV-2,5T used to unwind the raw material.
A1 - The machine has a unit called vertical platform used to contain the raw material.
A2 - The machine has a unit called vertical pay-off used to decoil the raw material.
English
27/403

--- page 30 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
B -
The machine has a unit called stretching / descaling unit STRETCH/SM MIRROR which carries out the
following operations:
‒
Wire stretching process:
The unit reduces the wire section with special rollers.
‒
Wire drawing process:
The unit removes in the wire to be rolled the surface layer of oxide that is formed during the hot rolling
in steel mill.
C -
The machine has a unit called DL +PSC MIRROR material treatment unit used to prepare the surface
of the raw material of its processing.
C1 - The machine has a unit called rolling cassette holder used to house the work tool (Rolling cassette).
D -
The machine has a group called MO 2P+SN+ST MIRROR horizontal monobloc used for pulling the
rebar.
E -
The machine has a unit called PCS 2 MIRROR rolling cassette holder used to house the work tool
(Rolling cassette).
F -
The machine has a straightener RETA DRIVE 4/16 MIRROR cULus that moves, straightens, measures
and cuts the wire.
G -
The machine has a bench BSR/18 MIRROR for the support of the handwork under construction and
temporary storage (Unloading bench).
H -
The machine has a roller conveyor called a transfer track PT/1/23 H=870 designed to move the
finished product.
I -
The machine has a control console from which its operating functions are activated or deactivated.
J -
The machine has an power cabinet for the supply of electrical energy to the electric components of the
machine.
K -
The machine has a hydraulic drive unit for supplying energy to the hydraulic cylinders.
L -
The machine has a unit called a rolling cassette
that rolls the wire.
2.1.8. FUNCTIONING CYCLE AND FUNCTIONING WAY OF THE MACHINE/PLANT
The main processing steps are described below:
The following operations are delegated to the operator:
‒
The operator carries out the programmino of the work cycle.
The following operations are entrusted to the operator responsible for loading the raw material:
‒
The operator loads on the pay-off the raw material having appropriate diameter and size.
The following operations are delegated to the maintenance operator:
‒
The operator makes the tooling of the machine/plant according to the work cycle to be carried out.
The following operations are delegated to the operator:
‒
The operator inserts the raw material inside the machine/plant.
The following describes the automatic working cycle in its main stages.
The following operations are entrusted to the operator responsible for loading the raw material:
‒
The operator starts the automatic working cycle.
The machine/system will perform the work cycle set.

2.2. MACHINE IDENTIFICATION
|
Note
In some texts, for editorial reasons, the STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR is identified with the
word "MACHINE / PLANT" or simply "MACHINE".
Whenever communicating with the manufacturer, always give the serial number of the machine, which
is found on the identification plate.
The identification plate, which is fixed on the control console or machine side, gives the following
details:
A -
Machine (Type of machine).
B -
Model (Specific model).
C -
Serial number (Serial number).
D -
Year of manufacture.
E -
Required pneumatic pressure (Pneumatic pressure necessary to power the machine/system).
F -
kg (Overall machine weight).
G -
Nominal tension (Supply voltage).
H -
Frequency (Power supply frequency for alternate voltage).
I -
Number of phases (Number of phases that feed the machine/system).
L -
Current at full load (Sum of the full load currents of the motors and other equipment that can work
simultaneously under normal conditions of use).
J -
Short circuit current (Overcurrent value that occurs due to a short circuit owing to a fault or an incorrect
connection in an electronic circuit).
K -
Electrical plan (Wiring diagram code).
M -
Weight (Weight of the electrical cabinet).

‒

‒
The values given were measured with the machine in standard working configuration with all safety
devices correctly installed.
‒
Taking into account that the machine’s noise emission depends on the diameter of the iron rebar being
processed, the processing operation carried out during measurement is representative for the maximum
noise level that the machine can generate during operation.
|
Note
The aerial noise values indicated are emission levels and do not represent safe operational levels.
Despite the fact that there is a relationship between emission levels and exposure levels, such relationship
cannot be used in a reliable way to determine whether further precautions are necessary or not.
The factors determining the level of exposure to which workers are submitted, include duration of exposure,
workroom characteristics and other sources of noise (number of machines, adjacent processes etc).
Furthermore, the max. permissible levels of exposure may vary from country to country.
However, the above-mentioned details will enable the user to carry out a better assessment of the danger and
the risk to the workforce.
English
70/403

|
Note
For this noise-level test we did not consider the inevitable breaks that usually occur during a normal work day
for tooling, adjustment, etc. and which decerease significantly the average acoustic pressure level.

machine is used.
‒

concerning safety and hygiene in the workplace, the client is responsible for satisfying the
requirements.

4.
CONTROLS
4.1. GENERAL WARNINGS REGARDING THE CONTROLS
The machine must not be operated if only this section has been read.
Operating the machine after reading only this section may lead to risks and serious damage.
Before operating the machine it is absolutely essential to have read and understood the entire manual.
Always keep the machine’s controls in perfect working order.
Keep the controls identification plates always perfectly legible.
Do not place drinks and containers with liquids on the control console or on other electrical equipment in order
to avoid the risk of electrocution in the event of spillage.
In the event of a fault replace the faulty parts with genuine spares.
i Warning
The presence of unidentified commands leads to the risk of incorrect machine operations.
The main controls of the machine/system are described below. All the other controls are indicated and
described in the software user manual.

4.3. DESCRIPTION OF THE CONTROLS IN "TANDEM FUNCTION"
The "tandem function" represents the synchronized movement of organs in the machine.
The elements that can be moved to "tandem function" are those that are activated with the local control of
"Motor activation selector".
Below are the controls that can activate the tandem function of the machine:
‒
Selector forward/backward pulling unit capstan.
‒
Forward/backward pulling selector.
‒
Blades rotation selector.
The elements controlled in "tandem function" perform the movements according to the functions of the
enabled commands (The organs actually enabled for tandem operation are those directly consecutive or
directly preceding where the local command "Engine Activation Selector" is in "ON" position).
By operating any tandem-enabled control, synchronous handling of the involved elements is obtained.
4.4. DESCRIPTION OF THE CONTROLS
OF THE CONSOLE
The controls available on the control console
are shown and described below.
A -
TOUCH-SCREEN MONITOR PANEL.
Display interface and selection of operations
that are set and running.
B -
ON/OFF SWITCH.
Two-position key switch ON (operating) - OFF
(not-operating).
The switch turns on or off the power supply to
the operative functions of the machine.
The key may be removed when it is in the OFF
position.
GRUPPO
C -
RESET BUTTON.
Blue luminous pressure button.
When the light is on, it indicates that the machine/system is not working; when it is off, the
machine/system is ready for use.
When the light is on it is necessary to reset the settings to go on working.
D -
SERIAL PORT.
Always active serial port that enables connection of external devices (bar codes reader and PC) to the
machine computer.

BRANO
E -
NORMAL WORKING BUTTON.
White light push-button.
The control is active when the machine is in the running state.
The drive of this control allows the start of the work cycle at the speed set by the operator.
The pilot light is on when the machine makes an automatic work cycle.
F -
QUICK STOP BUTTON.
Red pushbutton.
The command is on when the machine/plant makes an automatic cycle.
The drive of this control allows rapid stop of the work cycle.
The restarting of the working cycle occurs by pressing the "Start" button.
G -
SLOW WORKING SELECTOR.
White luminous two stable positions selector.
The drive of this control enables the start of the low speed work cycle (Automatic cycle).
The pilot light is on when the machine makes an automatic work cycle.
H -
NORMAL STOP BUTTON.
Red pushbutton.
The command is on when the machine/plant makes an automatic cycle.
The drive of this control allows the stop of the work cycle.
The machine will stop after a preset time.
The restarting of the working cycle occurs by pressing the "Start" button.
I -
ENGINE ACTIVATION OFF/ON SELECTOR
(PULLING UNIT CAPSTAN).
White luminous two stable positions selector.
The control is active when the machine is in the
running state.
The light is on steadily when the control is in
position "ON".
The pilot light is off when the control is in
position "OFF".
The (I) control enables or disables the
consensus of the handling of the element
operated by the (I1) control (according to the
functions of the (I1) control).
The (I) control enables the (I1) operation in
tandem control (in accordance with the
functions of the (I1) control).
GRUPPO
J -

Manipulator 4 unstable positions.
The control is active when the machine is in the running state.

use manual).

Description of the controls of the
bright device
The following describes the composition and
meaning of the individual elements:
A -
EMERGENCY SIGNALING LAMP.
Red indicator light.
When the element emits light, it indicates that
the machine/system is in emergency state.
B -
FAULT SIGNALING LAMP.
Yellow indicator light.
When the element emits light, it indicates that
the machine/system has detected a
malfunction.
GRUPPO
C -
SIGNALING LAMP OF MACHINE IN WORKING STATE.
Green indicator light.
- The lamp pilot light does not emit light:
The operator can execute through the controls of the single-handligs machine.
- The element emits intermittent light.
The element emits a signal for a 3 sec time before the machine makes movements determined by the
start of the automatic cycle.
The signaling allows all exposed people to move away from the danger zone or alternatively to prevent
the start-up of the machine by means of a control for the emergency stop located in the dangerous
area.

STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
5.
SAFETY DEVICES
5.1. WARNING ABOUT THE SAFETY DEVICES
Safety devices can be mechanical, electrical or of other type, their aim is to prevent machine elements
(mechanical members) from operating, until the element is not correctly restored.
i Warning
It is prohibited to tamper the safety devices.
Usually installed devices are described.
The machine can be equipped of additional safety devices for special conditions or specific requests of the
customer.
5.2. USEFUL LIFE OF THE SAFETY DEVICE
The useful life of a safety device is 20 years,unless otherwise indicated in the schemes and diagrams book.
Replace the safety device before the end of its useful life(EN ISO 13849-1).
i Warning
Replace the end-of-life safety device with a new one with similar features(EN ISO 13849-1).
Make the machine inoperative by setting it in the off mode until the device is replaced at the end of its life.
The safety device to be replaced before the end of its useful life is shown below:
‒
Interlock device.
‒
Optoelectronic device with active protection (ODPA).
‒

immediately if necessary.

5.9. PROCEDURES FOR ACCESS TO ZONES WITH FIXED GUARDS
To prevent access to danger zones, the various parts of the plant are fitted with protections formed by
elements (steel sheeting, steel mesh, plastic material etc.) fixed with threaded connections (screws
and/or bolts).
To avoid the risk of serious injury due to the presence of moving parts adhere strictly to the following
rules of conduct:
‒
The protection devices must always be present and correctly fixed with screws during operation of the
machine.
‒
The protection devices must only be removed by authorised personnel, using suitable tools.
‒
The protection devices must only be removed when the machine is stopped and when it is not possible
for it to be restarted by third parties.
‒
Before removing fixed guards disconnect the machine from the power supply and attach the sign “”work
in progress; do not make any operation”.
‒
Always fit a personal lock to the main switch in the OFF position to avoid the risk of accidental starting
by third parties when the switch is in this position.
‒
Before removing the protection devices check that all moving parts are completely still; parts with a
high inertia may sometimes continue to move for some time after the stop command has been given.
‒
Before restarting the machine/plant reposition the protection devices and lock them with all the
threaded fittings planned by the Manufacturer.
The threaded fasteners must be torqued in such a way as to prevent their removal using only the hands or
makeshift tools.
Do not remove and/or tamper the protections or safety devices fitted to the machine or the perimeter.
Regularly check the correct operation and report any operational faults to your boss.
English
105/403

--- page 108 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
i Warning
If guards and/or protections are removed or tampered with it becomes possible to access dangerous parts of
the machine with the resulting risk of serious injury.

6.
SAFETY REQUIREMENTS
6.1. GENERAL SAFETY PRECAUTIONS
This section must be fully read and understood before starting any operation on the machine.
Ordinary safety requirements included in this section regulate the relationship between the operator and the
machine.
It is also necessary to comply to the safety requirements indicated in the sections and to the requirements
specific to the single operations, to safeguard the operator and the machine by risks.
i Warning
In drawing up the “corporate safety plan” in observance of the laws in force regarding safety in the workplace,
take into account all the information contained in these “instructions for use” as well as the safety regulations,
in particular regarding “training and information” for the operators, above all regarding the residual risks and
posting the necessary warning signs.
6.2. OPERATOR SUITABILITY FOR USE
The suitability of the machine for the performance of the activities must be checked in advance with
health and safety inspections, in accordance with the current regulations in the country of use.
Even after satisfactory health and safety inspections, the machine must not be operated by persons
who habitually take or have taken:
‒
Alcoholic beverages/drinks.
‒
Drugs.
‒
Medication that may reduce the level of alertness and in relation to which the use of machines and
driving of a vehicle are expressly prohibited during treatment.
The machine must not be operated by individuals with abnormal physical conditions.
i Warning
Suitable physical and mental conditions are absolutely essential for the safe use of the machine.
6.3. OPERATOR INFORMATION AND TRAINING
The operators must be adequately trained in accordance with the provisions of current local legislations before
operating on the machine.
The first training course is held at the time of installation by personnel authorised by the Manufacturer and it is
recommended that more than one operator participate in the course.
Notice
The Manufacturer is, however, available to arrange additional courses upon request.
The information and training course of the personnel must be recorded as per the provisions of the local
applicable regulations relating to safety of work environments.
Schnell declines all liability resulting from the failure to perform training of its own personnel.
Subsequently the training can be performed by the operators trained by the Manufacturer during the first
course, if the owner considers they have the required skills to transmit the know-how.
The training course consolidates the knowledge gained from the “Instructions for use” manual.
A good knowledge of the machine and its operating modes is required in order to operate on the machine.
English
107/403

--- page 110 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
All the safety prescriptions and operating instructions given in this publication and in publications concerning
installed accessories, and any update bulletins transmitted by the manufacturers, must be observed at all
times.
The operators must also strictly adhere to local legislations in force regarding health and safety in the
workplace.
Training, skills and the specific qualifications for the individual activities as:
‒
Operations relating to load lifting.
‒
Operations, relating to the troubleshooting, replacement and adjustment of electrical equipment.
‒
Operations on the pneumatic system.
‒
Operations on the oil hydraulic system.
‒
Etc…
They are pertaining to the employer.
Moreover, the operators must possess the required skills, relating to all residual risks, pertaining to their
specific activity.

Only the operator is authorised to enter the operator area.

‒

Ordinary maintenance (According to section Maintenance).
6.5. SURVEILLANCE OF THE MACHINE
Before starting the machine and during operation always check that there are no people in danger
zones, especially inside the segregated areas and close to zones where the materials are being
processed.
i Warning
‒
Do not start the machine if people are present in danger zones.
‒
Stop the machine if, during operation, people approach the danger zones.
‒
The machine must always be under the supervision of a trained operator, even when working in
automatic mode.

6.6. GENERAL WARNING FOR THE USE OF THE MACHINE
i Warning
‒
The operator is not authorised to use the tools contained in the toolbox.
‒
The operator is not authorised to carry out machine/system tooling/maintenance operations or to use
the special wrenches unless expressly indicated.
‒
The maintenance technician is not authorised to load the raw materials, to carry out operations related
to processing or operating cycles, process control, or finished product unloading.
‒
The operating devices cannot be rendered completely safe (Rebar pay-off, rebar insertion, etc.) as this
might affect the efficiency of the system.
Therefore the machine presents serious residual risks of cutting, shearing, crushing, entanglement,
knocking and abrasion.
It is therefore obligatory for the operator to be appropriately trained to use the machine, and he will
have to be extremely careful during its use.
‒
It is prohibited to stand in the processing area and even more to lean on the machine, when the
machining is in progress.
It is strictly forbidden to enter the “processing area” for any reason during processing for the operator
and anyone else, animals included.
This point must be clearly pointed out by posting an appropriate warning sign in accordance with local
laws in force regarding industrial health and safety.
‒
If for any reason the machine shows functioning defects of any kind, especially the parts linked to safety
of the machine, immediately interrupt operation and disconnect the machine from all energy sources
until complete return to full functionality.
In case of doubt, do not proceed by trying improvising solutions, but consult the Manufacturer directly.
‒
When the machine is in motion or coupled to machines in motion, do not touch the raw materials being
processed or any moving part with your hands.
Even when the machine is off, never touch the rebar or any moving part with your hands, but use tools,
especially in the rebar passage areas between the straightening wheels and all the internal devices of
the machine. Be particularly careful during rebar insertion.
‒
Pay close attention to any outside personnel near the machine/system.
This point must be clearly pointed out by posting an appropriate warning sign in accordance with local
laws in force regarding industrial health and safety.
‒
Wear individual protection equipment (IPE).
‒
It is forbidden to use the machine for uses other than those for which it was designed.
‒
Always use the machine with due attention.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions.
‒
Do not make any manoeuvres of which you do not fully know the consequences.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions.
‒
Keep the work area tidy and free from obstructions (Untidiness can cause accidents).
‒
Never run near the machine/line. Walk normally.
‒
Always complete work cycles in order to free the machine/line of raw materials and finished goods.
English
109/403

--- page 112 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
Do not switch off or tamper with the safety microswitches installed on the machine/line. Ensure their
proper working order in accordance with the maintenance programme.
‒
Carefully read and understand the instructions for use of the machine/line software, provided as an
annex to this manual, before setting up the data for processing.
‒
Never leave objects and/or finished products in the work area, passage ways or on the machine/line.
‒
If the machine/system breaks down or malfunctions, especially if the fault involves the safety
components, stop the machine/system immediately and disconnect it from power sources.
Then remove all the copies of the keys that enable it to be used, until the fault is completely rectified.

quantity sufficient to do the installation and tests.
‒
Before using the machine, regardless of whether it has been installed by our technician, check that
there is oil in the gearmotors and the lubricators and that the level is at maximum allowed.
6.7. WORKING CLOTHES
Always wear suitable clothes before approaching and/or starting activities on the machine.
During performance of the working activity the following precautions must be taken:
‒
Wear working clothes without loose parts which may get caught up and dragged in by the moving and
rotating parts of the machine.
‒
Wear clothes with appropriate fastening systems (buttons, zips, velcro, etc.) and always fasten them.
‒
Do not wear clothes with wide sleeves, hanging belts, not properly fastened braces.
‒
Do not wear scarves, ties, etc.
‒
Do not wear sweaters, aprons and similar garments hanging on the shoulders or tied around the waist.
‒
Do not wear necklaces, chains, bracelets, rings or watches.
‒
Tie back long hair before working with the machine.

‒
Risks arising from dust and gas emission.

‒
Danger of falling objects or material.

‒
Risk of exposure to noise.

6.9. RESIDUAL RISKS AND RULES OF CONDUCT
There are the following residual risks during operation of the machine and the precautions listed below must
be adopted.
The following residual risks are present inside the working area (see section Safety devices) of the machine.
6.9.1. GENERAL RISKS
Here below are the residual risks common to the various activities, performed both by the operator and by the
maintenance technician.
•
Risk of slipping and falling
There may be materials on the floor near the machine which may cause risks of slipping.
The floor may have traces of hydraulic oil or lubrication of the guide rails due to small leaks, with
consequent risk of slipping.
How to behave:
‒
Therefore, always keep the floor near the operator station clean.
‒
Always wear safety shoes with anti-slip soles.
‒
Walk with caution in order to identify any slippery points.
English
111/403

--- page 114 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
•
Risk of stumbling and tripping
Material may fall to the ground when using the machine, with the consequent risk of tripping over it.
How to behave:
‒
Therefore, always keep the floor near the operator station clean.
‒
Always keep areas in which workers will be operating or passing through clear from encumbrances and
miscellaneous materials.
‒
Always proceed at a walking pace, paying attention to the possible presence of obstacles.
•
Risk for lack of illumination
The machine is not fitted with its own lighting so ample lighting must be provided to the work area,
avoiding the formation of shadowy areas, glare and stroboscopic effects.
The user is responsible for preparing an adequate lighting system in order to prevent any risk.
Arrange at least a lighting of 500 lux in the whole operator area, the work area, the segregated area, in
order to avoid hazardous situations.
In case of poor lighting, stop the process in progress in safety conditions and inform the maintenance
service in order to restore lighting.
Do not perform any operation, until at least the minimum lighting level required by this manual has
been restored.
•
Fire risk
Fire outbreaks in electrical equipment can develop extensively.
How to behave:
‒
Comply with the indications regarding the risk of fire and fire prevention measures.
‒
Always make sure that the electrical equipment is in perfect condition and repair any damage.
‒
If a fire starts disconnect power by setting the all-pole main disconnect switch to OFF and then use
extinguishers that are compatible with electrical fires.
•
Risks of fall from elevated positions
During the stay, ascent and descent from elevated positions, falls may occur.
How to behave:
‒
Use an appropriate equipment to work in height safety.
‒
Always keep the steps and the floor clean and tidy, always hold on with both hands when climbing or
getting off.
‒
Do not climb over the machine or any part of it for any reason, even if it is open and not connected to
power supply sources.
•
Risks due to extreme temperatures
The operator may be exposed to risks in the various machine/system operating phases arising from
contact with the hot surface of the metal wire or of the adjacent metal parts.
The operator may also be exposed to risks arising from contact with the surface of the metal wire during
loading/unloading.
Maintenance technicians may be exposed to the same risks when working in the danger zone or after
removing a fixed or movable guard.
The above mentioned residual risks arising from contact with the hot metal wire or adjacent metal parts
can be eliminated by using personal protective equipment, such as heat-proof gloves, intended for the
operator and the maintenance operator.
English
112/403

--- page 115 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
•
Risk arising from use of not suitable raw material
The use of materials with other characteristics may adversely affect the correct operation of the
machine and result in risks for the operator.
How to behave:
‒
Carry out the processing of rebars with technical characteristics described in the Section “Technical
data".
‒
The raw material being processed must neither be wet nor rusty. This would be harmful to both the
machine and the safety of the operator. Dripping poses a risk of electrocution. For this reason the
machine or accessories cannot be installed in open, unprotected spaces.
i Warning
The safety, reliability and operating capacity of the machine is guaranteed only if all the instructions
given by the manufacturer are respected, and if the raw material being processed has the above
mentioned characteristics and does not have diffused or localised defects or faults.
Notice.
The use of wet raw material causes the oxidation and thus the crumbling of the component.
•
Risks arising from dust and gas emission
The emission of dusts and suspended particles occurs due to the following cause:
‒
Detachment of slags from the rebar.
‒
Lubricant issued by the lubrication device.
For the operator and maintenance operator, there is a residual risk resulting from the contact or suction
of dust and particles that remain in suspension as a result of the activities carried out.
The elimination of residual risk is obtained by adopting appropriate protection measures:
‒
Carefully read the safety data sheet of the product used.
‒
Use of the Personal Protective Equipment (PPE):
- Protective mask for the respiratory tract.
- Glasses with side screens.
- Gloves.
- Protective garments.
‒
Adopt appropriate activities and behavioral cautions (eg factory safety procedures) to prevent the
suction of harmful and dangerous substances.
•
Risks due to dry rolling lubricant contact
For the operator and maintenance operator, there is a residual risk resulting from the contact or suction
of dust and particles that remain in suspension as a result of the activities carried out.
The elimination of residual risk is obtained by adopting appropriate protection measures:
‒
Carefully read the safety data sheet of the product used.
‒
Use of the Personal Protective Equipment (PPE):
- Protective mask for the respiratory tract.
- Glasses with side screens.
- Gloves.
- Protective garments.
‒
Adopt appropriate activities and behavioral cautions (eg factory safety procedures) to prevent the
suction of harmful and dangerous substances.
English
113/403

--- page 116 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
•
Risk generated by the use of controls on the machine

How to behave:

Do not touch the moving parts.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions.
‒
Do not make any manoeuvres of which you do not fully know the consequences.
‒

‒
Do not touch the “raw material”.
‒
Do not touch the product being implemented.
‒

Do not touch the moving parts.

Do not touch the “raw material”.

Do not touch the product being implemented.

Remain outside of the expected path/development of the product being implemented.
6.9.2. RISKS DUE TO THE HANDLING OF A LOAD
The following are the residual risks.
•
Risk when manually handling loads
Manually handling the raw materials to be machined, finished products, tooling components and spare
parts may lead to back/lumbar injury.
How to behave:
‒
Wear protective gloves.
‒
Wear protective shoes (see PPD).
‒
Avoid lifting loads heavier than those evaluated as risky by your employer.
•
Risk related to load handling using lifting equipment
The fall of a load causes the risk of crushing.
How to behave:
‒
The operator must be enabled to use the used lifting means.
‒
Carefully follow the “safety standards for transportation and handling” during handling operations.
‒
Perform the hooking and lifting operation according to the Manufacturer’s instructions.
‒
Lifting and transport must be carried out with means suitable for the weight to be lifted and
transported.
‒
Use a lifting equipment suitable for the load to be lfited.
‒
Sling the load properly.
‒
Make sure that no part of the load falls during lifting or transport.
‒
When lifting the load, make sure that the lifting brackets are securely fastened.
‒
The load must be free, without obstructions.
‒
Wear a safety helmet.
‒
Wear protective gloves.
English
114/403

--- page 117 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
Wear non-slip safety shoes with reinforced toes.
‒
Lift loads as little as possible from the ground.
‒
Move the load slowly, without causing bumps or jolts.
‒
The load must be under the control of the operator at all times.
‒
Do not come close with the body to any parts or materials which may fall.
‒
Do not make any manoeuvres of which you do not fully know the consequences.
‒
Make sure that the load, when repositioned to the ground, has a stable balance and is positioned in a
support plane appropriate to the weight to be sustained.

•
Risk of loss of control of the raw
material being processed
During the handling of the raw material it is
possible to lose the control of it.
There are residual risks of impact, grasping,
dragging, crushing, shearing, abrasion, object
projection, "whiplash", etc.
How to behave:
―
Always stand on the side opposite (external) to
the coil rebar unwinding direction;
i Warning
The correct position of the operator is the one
shown in the illustration.
GRUPPO
―
Only operate one coil at a time, according to the section Use and do not start to operate on another
unless the operation has concluded;
―
Tightly hold the end part of the raw material. When required, move it using only your hands, otherwise
secure it appropriately;
―
Before releasing the end part of the raw material, secure it appropriately;
―
Do not touch the unwound raw material and the parts (pay-off spindle, coil holder, roller, ...) in contact
with the same as uncontrollable potential energy is stored within them;
―
Always keep and maintain the raw material at a distance from your body and head;
―
Always pay the utmost attention when manually handling;
―
Do not make any manoeuvres of which you do not fully know the consequences;
―
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions;
‒
Wear individual protection equipment (IPE).

6.9.7. RISKS DUE TO ADJUSTMENTS
It is prohibited to modify the calibration of the devices without authorisation.
The residual risks related to machine/system adjustment are listed below.
•
Risk of falling of the raw material
During the processing the breakage of the rebar may occur due to insufficient lubrication, excessive
reduction of the section or defects of the raw material used.
How to behave:
‒
Do not approach the handling area of the rebar being processed.
‒
Do not enter the segregated area.
•
Risk of whiplash
During the processing the breakage of the rebar may occur due to insufficient lubrication, excessive
reduction of the section or defects of the raw material used.
The breaking of the raw material could cause dangerous fluctuations with consequent whiplash.
How to behave:
‒
Do not enter the segregated area.
‒
Do not enter inside the work area.
‒
Do not approach the handling area of the rebar being processed.
English
119/403

--- page 122 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
•
Risk of projecting objects
During the processing the breakage of the rebar may occur due to insufficient lubrication, excessive
reduction of the section or defects of the raw material used.
The breaking of the raw material could cause dangerous fluctuations with consequent whiplash.
The whiplash of the raw material can throw objects in the air.
How to behave:
‒
Do not enter the segregated area.
‒
Do not enter inside the work area.
‒
Do not approach the handling area of the rebar being processed.
•
Risk of loss of control of the raw material being processed
How to behave:
‒
Press the emergency stop buttons if a dangerous situation occurs.
6.9.8. RISKS DUE TO THE AUTOMATIC CYCLE
Here below are the residual risks, pertaining to the automatic cycle of the machine/plant.
•
Residual risks due to the presence of working elements and raw material being
processed
The processing devices act directly on the raw material being processed.
The following are the residual risks:
Residual risks of crushing, cutting, abrasion, dragging, objects projection, “whiplashes”, etc.
As the rebar makes a wide movement during the processing phases, it is not possible to apply a cover
that fully protects the potentially hazardous areas determined by the movement of both the operating
devices and the rebar.
Therefore a segregated and working area has been defined in which it is strictly prohibited for anybody
to enter (operator included) during the machine/plant operation.
How to behave:
‒
Keep away from the processing device areas or the areas in which the rebar being machined moves.
‒
Keep the hands away from the operating devices and the raw material input area and from the area
swept by the piece being formed.
‒
Do not touch the “raw material”.
‒
Do not touch the product being implemented.
‒
Remain outside of the expected path/development of the product being implemented.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions.
‒
Therefore a segregated and working area has been defined in which it is strictly prohibited for anybody
to enter (operator included) during the machine/plant operation.
‒
Carefully observe the safety signs shown on the pages of this manual and the safety pictograms applied
on the machine.
‒
Use the machine only after having carefully read the “instructions for use” and in particular the safety
regulations and after having attended a proper informing and training course.
‒
Press the emergency stop buttons if a dangerous situation occurs.
‒
Do not make any manoeuvres of which you do not fully know the consequences.
‒
The operator must always place himself within the operator area.

•
Residual risks due to the continuation of the processing cycle
It is possible to continue the processing cycle welding the ends of the rebar (tails) of the raw material.
As the rebar makes a wide movement during the processing phases, it is not possible to apply a cover
that fully protects the potentially hazardous areas determined by the movement of both the operating
devices and the rebar.
Therefore a segregated and working area has been defined in which it is strictly prohibited for anybody
to enter (operator included) during the machine/plant operation.
The moment in which it is necessary to continue the work cycle with the loading of the raw material to
be processed is excluded (See section Use, subject "Continuation of the processing cycle").
The following are the residual risks that determine the progress of the operation:
Residual risks of crushing, cutting, abrasion, dragging, objects projection, “whiplashes”, etc.
How to behave:
‒
Keep away from the processing device areas or the areas in which the rebar being machined moves.
‒
Keep the hands away from the operating devices and the raw material input area and from the area
swept by the piece being formed.
‒
Immediately release the grip from the object before any movement and exit the processing area.
‒
Do not touch the product being implemented.
‒
Remain outside of the expected path/development of the product being implemented.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may be carried out in safety conditions.
‒

on the machine.

Use the machine only after having carefully read the “instructions for use” and in particular the safety
regulations and after having attended a proper informing and training course.

How to behave:

6.9.11.RISKS DURING THE MAINTENANCE
During maintenance, in addition to electrical hazards, there are also residual risks related to the presence of
fluids under pressure.
The maintenance technician must be trained on the specific operations assigned to him and must be
instructed on the risks associated with them.
The fixed guards not permanently welded to the machine are fastened with screws and require the use of
special wrenches to remove them. They may only be removed by the persons in charge of maintenance, using
the appropriate wrench.
Only maintenance technicians may access the compartments protected by a fixed guard.
The operator may never under any circumstances attempt to open a fixed guard.
•
Risk of crushing
Manually handling rods and finished products may pose a risk of crushing the upper and lower limbs.
How to behave:
‒
Wear safety shoes with supporting rod.
‒
Use cut-proof gloves.
•
Cutting risk for rebar manual handling
Manual handling of rods that could have sharp “burrs”, presents the risk of cutting with consequent
possibility of injury.
How to behave:
‒
Always pay the utmost attention when manually handling.
‒
Use cut-proof gloves.
English
124/403

--- page 127 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
•
Risk of electrocution
The machine is manufactured in accordance with the current safety regulations regarding electrical
power supply.
There is an electric shock hazard in the case of damage to cables and electrical equipment, with the
associated risk of serious or fatal injury.
How to behave:
‒
Always check that electrical cabinets are in perfect condition and properly closed, and that power
cables, cablepressers, and electrical equipment are in perfect condition.
‒
Inform the company’s maintenance service immediately in case of damage.
•
Risk of projection of fragments/splints
The use of compressed air for cleaning operations results in the projection of various materials with the
risk of eye injuries.
How to behave:
‒
Wear goggles or polycarbonate visors.
‒
Preferably use an industrial aspirator.
‒
Do not place the outlet near the face, both in the case of compressed air and air suction.
•
Risk of elements in pressure
Even if the pneumatic and hydraulic supply sources are disconnected, some components remain under
pressure.
The fluid contained within the tubes can keep the mechanical element in suspension or loaded.
Valves with closed centres are fitted in the machine/system.
How to behave:
‒
Do not loosen or disconnect the pipe before making sure that the internal fluid pressure has been
eliminated.
‒
Do not disconnect the parts under pressure.
‒
The operation must be carried out by adequately trained and qualified persons.
‒
Contact the technical support service Schnell before taking any action.
Pressure accumulator.
The operation must be carried out by adequately trained and qualified persons.
i Danger
Do not perform welding, brazing or mechanical work on the pressure accumulator.
Danger of explosion in case of welding.
Danger of explosion in case of brazing.
•
Risks due to extreme temperatures
The operator can be exposed to residual risks due to contact with surfaces or fluids having extreme
temperatures (> 43 °C).
How to behave:
‒
Keep an appropriate safety distance from the energy source.
‒
Check that heat is dispersed by the machine until cooled enough to touch prior to use.

7.
MACHINE/PLANT STATUS
7.1. ASSUMPTIONS FOR MACHINE / PLANT OPERATIONAL STATUS
The machine/system can go in one of the following operating states:
‒
Machine off.
‒
Machine in momentary stop.
‒
Machine in operation.
The operator has always to be there for the control and the management of the machine/plant in safety when
it is in either “temporary stop” or when the machine is “running”.
The machine/line area must be surrounded and marked out.
i Warning
Before operating the machine it is absolutely essential to have read and understood the entire manual.
7.2. OPERATIONAL STATUS (MACHINE OFF)
The machine/system is in “machine off” state if all the power supply sources (electrical, pneumatic, hydraulic,
etc.) have been disconnected and if the relative disconnect switches have been locked to prevent unexpected
restarting.
i Warning
Remaining residual risks due to the presence of stored potential energy (loaded springs, hot surfaces,
suspended loads, electric capacitors, tanks and / or pressure pipes, etc.) remain as described in the safety
requirements section.
Carry out the locking procedure to bring the machine to the "machine off" mode.
7.2.1. LOCKING PROCEDURE
Operate as follows:
Carry out the lock procedure before any use and maintenance intervention of the machine/plant.
Personally safeguard the padlock key.
i Warning
‒
Opening a protective device does not constitute locking.
‒
Use specific padlocks for the “lock-out tag-out” procedure (LOTO).

8.
TRANSPORT
8.1. ASSUMPTION FOR THE TRANSPORT AND THE HANDLING
The following section includes information concerning the machine handling and moving.
Always observe also all the general and specific regulations concerning lifting equipment and handling and
transport operations, including those that are not expressly stated in this manual.
Before operating the machine it is necessary to read and understand this manual, especially the section
“Safety instructions".
It is also necessary to comply to the safety requirements indicated in this section to safeguard the operator
and the machine by risks.
8.2. AUTHORIZED PERSONNEL TO THE HANDLING OF THE MACHINE
The only staff enabled for the handling of the machine is the authorized personnel SCHNELL.
Contact the technical support service SCHNELL before taking any action.
8.3. SAFETY PRESCRIPTIONS FOR TRANSPORT AND HANDLING OPERATIONS
The following chapter gives some rules which we would ask you to strictly adhere to in the interest of your own
safety.
Also comply to all the general and specific regulations regarding hoisting means and transport as well as
handling operations.
The lifting and transport must be carried out with accessories suitable for the weight to be lifted and
transported; carefully read the information in the packing-list.
NOTICE!
Our technicians, installing the machine, are not authorized to use hoisting systems nor, in terms of safety, to
supervise the work of third-parties!
The customer must therefore provide our technician with qualified personnel equipped with suitable lifting
means.
The manufacturer declines all liability related to the use of unsuitable lifting means.
Always use lifting accessories that are in a perfect status and suitable for the weight of the machine or the
parts to be lifted.
If any anomaly is detected in the lifting device, lifting accessories and lifting brackets of the machine/system,
do not try to perform any lifting operations and inform the person in charge immediately.
During machine handling and lifting operations, keep the manoeuvring area free of materials, objects and
people.
Operating lifting and transportation means must be entrusted to authorised personnel, qualified for their use.
During lifting and transport adopt the necessary precautions in order to prevent the part being handled from
falling and/or the means overturning.
NOTICE!
It is recommended to use accessories (beams or equalizers) which allow lifting the machine in vertical position
with suitable chains (or cables) at the points indicated.
If this is not possible, use suitable hoisting chains (or cables) in the less possible inclined position and
absolutely do not exceed the 45° limit.
Exceeding this limit will overload the chains (or cables) and the attachment areas which are not sized to
support such stress.
English
167/403

--- page 170 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
i Warning
‒
Before the lifting operation and transport, check carefully that there are no bodies that are free to move
in the load.
‒
Handle the machine/system very slowly and carefully since the loads are not balanced.
‒
Avoid bumping and impacts and lift the parts as little as possible above ground level.
‒
Adapt the acceleration and deceleration of vertical and horizontal traversing of the lifting device on the
basis of the weight and dimensions of the load;
Prevent the load from "jerking" downwards or oscillating horizontally due to
acceleration/deceleration/stopping of the lifting device.
The machine can be transported: on an open flatbed.
The machine/system is unbalanced.
Therefore, use cables or chains of adequate length and ensure that their capacity and that of the hoisting
means is suitable for the weight of the machine/system.
Adjust the length of the cables or chains in such a way that the machine/system is lifted parallel to the ground.
All staff, included the operator, will have to keep at a safe distance.
The maintenance technician must take into account the hazardous situation that may arise during an
unexpected event such as the breakage of a chain or eye-bolt and consequent overturning of the load.
i Danger
In the unfortunate event that a cable or chain snaps, they generate a violent and extremely powerful
“whiplash”.
No personal protection device is able to protect against this event.
Always bear this very high risk in mind and make sure that nobody is near the manoeuvring area and certainly
not standing in the direction of the cables or of the chains.
i Warning
Do not climb over the machine or any part of it for any reason, even if it is open and not connected to power
supply sources.
Before starting lifting operations ensure you are wearing the following personal
protective equipment (PPE):
ULTIMO GRUPPO TABELLA
‒
Hard hat.
‒
Cut-resistant gloves.
‒
Protective garments.
‒
Safety footwear with reinforced toecap and
nonslip sole.
GRUPPO

8.5. LIFTING SYSTEMS
The lifting and transport must be carried out by means of suitable equipment for the weights concerned,
taking into consideration the type of packaging and any specific instructions for individual parts.
Use fork trucks for lifting and transporting if the machine is shipped on a pallet with fork channels in the base.
Also in this case the machine must be handled by personnel who are qualified to use the lifting equipment in
question.
Use lifting systems with slinging of the load in the presence of specific attachment points for this purpose.
Carefully read the details given in the Section “Dimensions and weights” and in the packing list.
Some components are fitted with a plate which
indicates the total weight.
English
169/403

--- page 172 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
8.5.1. SUITABILITY OF THE LIFTING DEVICES
The lifting equipment must always be in perfect condition, fully functional, in compliance with statutory
regulations in force in the country of use, and used in compliance with the prescriptions of the manufacturer.
The lifting and transport gear must be operated by authorised personnel.
The lifting and transport must be carried out with plant and equipment suitable for the particular weight, to
choose the correct equipment carefully read the details given in the Section “Dimensions and weights of parts
to be lifted” paying particular attention to the weight of the part to be lifted and transported.
During lifting and transport adopt the necessary precautions in order to prevent the part being handled from
falling and/or the means overturning.
i Warning
The load capacity of the lifting device and the lifting accessories must be checked via the relevant plate
located on the said device.
GRUPPO
8.5.2. SUITABILITY OF THE LIFTING MEANS
The lifting accessories and the harnessing accessories must always be in excellent working order, suitable for
the weights to be lifted, compliant with the current regulations in the country of use and used in compliance
with the instructions given by the manufacturer and/or the relative technical standards.
The lifting and transport must be carried out with accessories suitable for the weight to be lifted and
transported.
In order to select the lifting accessories and the harnessing accessories, careful reference must be made to the
details given in the Section “Dimensions and weights of the parts to be lifted”, paying particular attention to
the weight of the part to be lifted and transported.
i Warning
The load capacity of the lifting device and the lifting accessories must be checked via the relevant plate
located on the said device.
8.5.3. USE OF EQUALIZERS
It is necessary to use accessories (beams or equalizers) which allow the machine to be lifted in vertical
position with suitable chains (or cables) at the points indicated.
English
170/403

--- page 173 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
8.5.4. USE OF SLINGS
If it is not possible to use equalizers, use lifting slings
made of steel ropes or chains.
In this case, it is absolutely essential that the angle
formed between the chains or ropes and the vertical,
it is not greater than 45°.
GRUPPO
i Danger
An angle wider than 45° determines a high solicitation both on the sling parts and on coupling points with an
high risk of damaging and breaking.
Exceeding this limit will overload the chains (or cables) and the attachment areas which are not sized to
support such stress.
8.6. WARNINGS FOR THE PRE-HANDLING
During lifting and transport keep with due consideration the dimension of the available spaces and the ground
features, particularly for the machines or the systems that are installed in a building yard.
During lifting procedures pay attention to obstacles in the area of manoeuvres.
Pay special attention to the presence of electrical power lines, especially on construction sites.
During machine handling and lifting operations, keep the manoeuvring area free of materials, objects and
people.
8.7. WARNINGS FOR THE HANDLING
The transport, lifting and assembly operations must be carried out by specialised companies working in the
machinery transport sector; it is only possible to perform the various operations in conditions of safety when
suitable skills are combined with the use of the correct equipment.
Parts secured to pallets can be handled by a forklift truck.
|
Note
Specific components (frames, supports, fasteners, etc.) are assembled on the machine to facilitate transport
and assembly operations.
Carefully preserve these components if machine disassembly, transport and assembly operations are
forecasted to be performed in the future.
During lifting procedures:
‒
Use the utmost caution.
‒
Keep all the people clear of the area of operations.
‒
Do not allow people to walk or stand under or near suspended loads.
‒
Lift loads as little as possible from the ground.
‒
Move the load slowly, without causing bumps or jolts.
‒
Keep the manoeuvre area free of materials and clutter.
‒
Use rods enough long for leading the load.
All staff, included the operator, will have to keep at a safe distance.
The maintenance technician must take into account the hazardous situation that may arise during an
unexpected event such as the breakage of a chain or eye-bolt and consequent overturning of the load.
English
171/403

--- page 174 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
8.8. INTENDED ATTACHMENT POINTS
i Warning
‒
The operator must be enabled to use the used lifting means.
‒
When lifting the machine/system, make sure that the lifting brackets are securely fastened.
‒
Carefully follow the “safety standards for transportation and handling” during handling operations.
‒
Perform the hooking and lifting operation according to the Manufacturer’s instructions.
‒
Lifting and transport must be carried out with means suitable for the weight to be lifted and
transported.
For the lifting and handling of parts, use the lifting points planned by the manufacturer, which are formed
generally by threaded holes for the fitting of eyebolts and lifting eyes, or, alternatively, if instructed by the
manufacturer, use lifting slings.
The lifting points may be easily identified as they are highlighted by a pictogram (see section “Safety
requirement”, chapter “Signs applied”).
i Danger
If the eyebolts (but also the hooks and chains, etc.) are damaged, they are very dangerous since they may
snap without forewarning. If the eyebolts are damaged (bent, stretched, dented, corroded, etc.) immediately
scrap them to prevent that others, unaware of the danger, use them. Never use eyebolts if you are not sure of
their integrity.
The individual parts of the machine which do not have lifting points indicated must be disassembled and lifted
by hand.
Follow the instructions given in this section and in the section “Safety requirement” during performance of this
operation.

8.25. POSITIONING OF THE PACKAGING OR MACHINE
THESE OPERATIONS ARE DANGEROUS AND MUST BE CAUTIOUSLY PERFORMED STEP BY STEP.
The control console is fixed to a pallet by means of straps and it is necessary to:
‒
Lift the pallet with a forklift truck.
‒
Move the pallet with the console to the area for installation of the machine.
‒
Place it near the area in which it will be permanently installed (ref. section “installation”).
‒
Cut the straps which fix the console to the pallet.
i Warning
Be careful of “whiplash” from the straps. The straps are sharp so cut-resistant gloves and protective goggles
must be worn.
The straps can cut, so always use special protective gloves and take care not to knock them with any other
part of the body.
‒
Lift it by means of the eyebolts using suitable lifting gear and accessories (ref. section “Suitability of
lifting gear” and “Suitability of lifting accessories and harnessing accessories”).
‒
Place the control console on the ground.
|
Note
Follow the same procedures described for the unpacking and positioning of the control console for all units or
parts fixed to pallets by means of straps.
i Warning
When lifting the machine/system, make sure that the lifting brackets are securely fastened.
Operate as follows:
‒
Hook the cables to the safety brackets and place the machine as indicated in the installation layout (See
section “Installation”).
‒
The machine must be installed on a flat surface in a stable position with all the contact surfaces
touching the floor.
‒
It is absolutely forbidden to raise the machine or install it on a slope using shims as this would make it
unstable with the consequent risk of overturning.
‒
Handle the control console and the containers
with units or tools, with cables, chains or with
the fork lift.
BRANO

183/403

‒
Check that there is a distance of at least 1,5 m from the wall for maintenance operations.
i Warning
‒
The unloading and positioning operations are dangerous and must be carried out with suitable and
efficient means, operating with the maximum caution.
‒
The capacity of these means must be suited to the maximum weight to be handled, also considering the
overall dimensions and the environment in which the machine will be operating.
‒
The handling operations are dangerous and must be carried out by expert personnel.

These machines are designed and built to work indoors; therefore before positioning the machine in the
manufacturing area, make sure that good quality, level industrial flooring is available with a minimum
guaranteed compression strength of no less than C 25 (25 RCK on stands = 250 kg/cm²).

8.28.2.REPACKING OF THE SPARE PARTS OF THE MACHINE / PLANT
The transport must be carried out in accordance with the load capacity of the lifting gear and the maximum
size allowed for transport.
When possible, restore the original packaging conditions, especially for those parts which can be fixed to and
moved on pallets.
Protect with specific products all surfaces, sliding pins, hinges and anything that can oxidize.
Protect the machine/plant or the units by means of protective covers.
Fix the parts which may be transported in this way to the pallet, by means of cables or straps.
Fix the safety signs to the packaging required to carry out the subsequent lifting and unpacking operations in a
safe manner.
Fit timber or cardboard spacers between the various units and fix them to the sides and between each other
with ties, cables, etc.
When possible, fix the units to the floor, with timber battens, so that the shaking caused by the transport does
not cause uncontrolled movement of the load.
8.29. LAYOUT OF UNITS ON MEANS OF TRANSPORT
The units in which the machine is subdivided have a loading order or a prearranged position inside the truck or
container.
The transport company is responsible for positioning.

9.
INSTALLATION
9.1. INTRODUCTION TO INSTALLATION PROCEDURES
Before operating the machine it is necessary to read and understand this manual, especially the section
“Safety instructions".
It is also necessary to comply to the safety requirements indicated in this section to safeguard the operator
and the machine by risks.
Technical staff of the Manufacturer is not authorised to make: the electrical connection of the machine to the
mains or connect the power supply cable.
These operations must be carried out at full expense and responsibility of the customer by specialised
personnel who will issue the necessary certifications in compliance with the local laws in force.
In the event of non-compliance with this requirement, the client will bear all civil and criminal responsibility for
any resulting consequences.
The manufacturer declines all liability for the possible consequences arising from failure to observe the
foregoing prescriptions.
9.2. AUTHORIZED PERSONNEL TO THE INSTALLATION OF THE MACHINE
The only staff enabled for the installation of the machine is the authorized personnel SCHNELL.
Contact the technical support service SCHNELL before taking any action.
9.3. SAFETY WARNINGS FOR INSTALLATION
i Warning
‒
Do not climb over the machine or any part of it for any reason, even if it is open and not connected to
power supply sources.
‒
It is absolutely forbidden to raise the machine or install it on a slope using shims as this would make it
unstable with the consequent risk of overturning.
‒
Unauthorised people must not be allowed into the working areas where these machines are used.
‒
Provide the work environment with fire extinguishers which are suitable for use on live electrical
equipment and which comply with the current local regulations.
‒
The machines are not to be used in open air, but only in industrial environments which are closed and
protected from the weather. This last point also goes for storage of the machine.
‒
The machine/system is not suitable for use in potentially explosive environments.
‒
Wear individual protection equipment
(IPE)
- Wear a safety helmet.
- Cut-resistant gloves.
- Oil-proof gloves.
- Protective garments.
- Wear safety footwear.
GRUPPO
English
187/403

--- page 190 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
9.4. ENVIRONMENTAL REQUIREMENTS FOR PLACING THE MACHINE
The work environment must have the following requirements:
‒
Properly protected from the weather.
‒
Cleared of all possible obstructions.
‒
Level industrial flooring with a minimum guaranteed compression strength of no less than C25 (25 RCK
on stands = 250  kg/cm²).
‒
The working environment temperature ha sto be between -5 °C and 40 °C.
‒
The lighting of the working environment must be at least 500 lux.
i Warning
‒
The machine is not fitted with its own lighting so ample lighting must be provided to the work area,
avoiding the formation of shadowy areas, glare and stroboscopic effects.
Arrange at least a lighting of 500 lux in the whole operator area, the work area, the segregated area, in
order to avoid hazardous situations.
‒
The working area of the machine/system must be appropriately signposted and marked off according to
the final installation layout.
‒
Flammable products and materials must not be kept near the machine.
‒
The working area is the minimum necessary. It may be increased in size but not reduced.
‒
Using the machine in explosive environments is strictly forbidden.
In the case of more stringent local legislation concerning “health and safety in the workplace” the work area
must be delimited as prescribed or required by the occupational health and safety inspectorate authorities.
For operational and safety reasons the control console must be positioned, as indicated in the layout, in order
to check that nobody enters the working area during operation of the machine.
Never position the control console where it interferes with the machine’s working area or where the machine
loads and unloads.
THE MACHINE/SYSTEM MUST BE INSTALLED FOLLOWING THE INDICATIONS OF THE FINAL
INSTALLATION LAYOUT.
THE MACHINE MUST NOT BE STARTED FOR ANY REASON WHATSOEVER IF THESE INSTRUCTIONS
ARE NOT COMPLIED WITH.
The manufacture is at your disposal for any technical information and/or to supply the necessary material. The
manufacturer expressly declines all liability for the consequences of failure to observe this warning.
i Warning
In the absence of physical/immaterial barriers (fences/photocells) or warning signs of the working areas
(yellow lines and white/red warning chains) the machine cannot be started.
THE MACHINE/SYSTEM MUST BE DELIMITED IN ACCORDANCE WITH THE SPECIFICATIONS INDICATED
IN THE FINAL INSTALLATION LAYOUT.
The manufacturer declines all liability for the possible consequences arising from failure to observe the
foregoing prescriptions.

9.7. WARNING FOR THE WORKS IN HEIGHT
i Warning
The means used to take the operator to the working height must comply with the instructions of the safety
regulation on workplaces as regards to “temporary works at heights”.
The mobile ladder (A) with stand or the access platforms (B) have the minimum useful features, to reach the
safety work height.
The ladders are considered mobile, when they can be moved where necessary.
GRUPPO
In order to comply with conformity criteria of the applicable regulation, the mobile ladders must be:
‒
Built according to the standard UNI EN 131 part one and two.
‒
Accompanied by a short description, featuring constituent elements as well as instructions for a correct
use for preservation and maintenance.
‒
Mark with the symbol 'EN 131' along with the name of the manufacturer, the type of ladder, year and
month of production, maximum permissible load, angle of inclination.
‒
Accompanied by conformity statements with technical standard.
‒
Ladders with a height, such as to stick out of the access surface by at least one metre.
‒
Closing system of the standing platform for the protection of the operator.
Notice
The CE marking placed on the ladder is not a guarantee of conformity with the standard UNI EN 131.
The precautions to comply with for the use of the ladder are the following:
‒
Closed shoes with non-slip sole, in order to avoid stumbling and slipping on the ladder steps.
‒
Always make sure the ladder is steadily fixed to the ground, before climbing on it.
‒
When climbing and getting off, always proceed with the face towards the ladder, making sure to
correctly place the foot on the step, especially when getting off.
‒
Keep your own centre of gravity as inside as possible to the standing platform, without unbalancing
outwards, in order to avoid the overturning of the ladder.
‒
Fasten with hooks or other systems the tools being used to impede their accidental fall.
English
190/403

--- page 193 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
Be careful when tools are used or when works, producing horizontal thrusts, which may have the ladder
slide on the floor, are carried out.
In order to counterbalance the thrusts, it is recommended to be supported by another person or perform
a fastening to a steady wall, which ensures a suitable anchoring.
‒
Do not place the ladder on irregular floors or near steep gradients.
The use of lifting means, different from the mobile ladder (fixed or mobile scaffolding, man baskets, car lifts,
etc.) entails the adoptions of other safety precautions.
The operator, performing maintenance
operations at heights (temporary works at
heights) must wear a “Safety belt with wire
rope” with the following features:
‒
It is necessary to use the safety belts with
straps and legs harness along with a suitable
wire rope, limiting the fall no more than 1,5 m
and ending with a safety carbine hook.
GRUPPO
‒
The use of the wire rope must occur along with energy absorbing devices (heat sinks), because even
falls from a medium height may cause high stopping power.
‒
The wire rope must be fastened, either directly or via a sliding ring along a suitably stretched rope, to
stable parts of the fixed or temporary works.
‒
The rope and all the constituent elements of the belt must have sections, so as to resist to stresses,
deriving from any fall of the worker.
‒
The length of the wire rope must be such as to limit the fall to no more than 1,50 m.
i Warning
Check the efficiency conditions of the protective device (PPE) before wearing it and that it bears the CE
marking on all its constituent elements.
|
Note
Receive the CE statement of conformity of the belts.
English
191/403

--- page 194 ---

9.18. POST-INSTALLATION CHECKS
Notice
For reasons of safety during transport, the machines are often shipped without oil or with a minimum quantity
sufficient to do the installation and tests.
Before using the machine/system, regardless of whether it has been installed by one of our technicians, check
that there is oil in the hydraulic control unit tank and that it is at maximum level.
i Warning
If for any reason the machine shows functioning defects of any kind, especially the parts linked to safety of the
machine, immediately interrupt operation and disconnect the machine from all energy sources until complete
return to full functionality.
In case of doubt, do not proceed by trying improvising solutions, but consult the Manufacturer directly.
|
Note
After the first 150 hours of operation, check that there is oil in the hydraulic control unit tank and that it is at
maximum level.

9.20. ELECTRICAL CONNECTION
The user must set up the electricity supply to the point requested by the Manufacturer (ref. installation layout
and electrical diagram).
Our technicians are not authorised to make the electrical connection of the machine/system, therefore the
Customer must provide for this operation autonomously at their own expense, using specialist staff.
The client must fit an appropriate earth cable and connector suitable for the purpose at its own expense.
Special attention must be paid when making connections that there is no obstacle to good electrical contact
(paint, rust, etc.) and that, if for any reason there is no earth cable coupling, the client shall provide a suitable
coupling for the purpose.
On completion of the connection operations, the client must make the appropriate test measurements with
special instruments and skilled personnel.
Do not connect the machine to any power supplies before completing the following operations.
I Caution
Check that the tension and frequency of the electrical
outlet correspond with those indicated on the
transformer and on the identification plate.
If envisioned by local laws in force, make sure suitable
differential switches for pulse currents and suitable
thermal magnetic circuit breakers are installed
upstream from the main electrical system and from
the machine/system power supply panel.
GRUPPO
The user must size the machine/system power supply and earthing according to the wiring
diagram.
|
Note
To make better use of the electric energy it is recommended to improve the power factor of the electric line
which feeds the machine, possibly with an automatic device.
The user of the machine is responsible for installing an electrical panel for connecting the machine to the
electric power line. The electrical panel must be composed by a magneto-thermal differential protected by
fuses.
If the electrical power supply has frequent power surges or black-outs, you will need a surge stabilizer or an
auxiliary electrical generator.
The cross-section indicated for the cables was calculated on the basis of the maximum current effectively
absorbed; consequently, every variation in control logic may modify this absorption and give rise to problems
related to insufficient cable cross-section, with the inevitable electrical risks (electric shock, fire, burns, etc).
To open the door of the control panel, and the door of the electrical panel, use the appropriate keys.

198/403

9.22. PNEUMATIC CONNECTION
The pneumatic connection requires a compressor with the following characteristics:
‒
An air dryer.
‒
A maximum pressure of 10 bar.
‒
According to section technical data.
‒
 ½" pipe couplings.
Notice
The air compressor must be fitted with a dryer.
The absence of this accessory may cause functioning irregularities and serious damage to the machine, for
which the manufacturer declines all responsibility.
|
Note
With low temperature, freezing of the condensate and the oil contained in the compressed air circuits might
cause malfunctioning of pneumatic movements.
An insufficient pressure and/or flow rate will certainly cause problems and malfunctions.

3

This operation must be carried out by a maintenance technician (The operator has to be suitably trained
and enabled for this type of intervention).
4
Disassemble in reverse order to assembly.
i Warning
If the machine consists of individual parts which may be dismantled, disassemble them and handle and
transport each part.
