10. USE
10.1. PREMISE FOR USING THE MACHINE / PLANT
Before operating the machine it is necessary to read and understand this manual, especially the section
“Safety instructions".
It is also necessary to comply to the safety requirements indicated in this section to safeguard the operator
and the machine by risks.
10.2. OPERATOR QUALIFIED TO USE THE MACHINE
Manning, management and use of the machine/line is the responsibility of a single operator.
Only the operator is authorised to enter the operator area.
Operator.
The operator is employed to the following operation:
‒
Raw material loading (According to section Use).
‒
Operations concerning processing or the operating cycles (According to section Use).
‒
Process control (According to section Use).
‒
Transfer of the finished product (According to section Use).
‒
Cleaning the walkways (According to section Maintenance).
Operator in charge of loading the raw materials.
The operator is employed to the following operation:
‒
Format change of the raw material (According to section Use).
‒
Continuation of work cycle (According to section Use).
‒
Cleaning the walkways (According to section Maintenance).
Maintenance operator.
The operator is employed to the following operation:
‒
Tooling of the machine (According to section Use).
‒
Finished product (According to section Maintenance).
‒
Ordinary maintenance (According to section Maintenance).

activated in the emergency state.

“Maintenance”, chapter “Lubrication.

The button/light “Reset” has the function to reset the initial setting values.
When the light is on it is necessary to reset the settings to go on working.
Press the “RESET” button/light:
‒
After every error message.
‒
After every emergency stop.
‒
When displayed on the monitor and the light is on.

10.10.SETUP
THE MACHINE/SYSTEM TOOLING OPERATION MUST BE CARRIED OUT BY A MAINTENANCE
TECHNICIAN.
i Warning
‒
Before re-starting the machine, correctly re-mount and tighten all parts which have been removed and
check that they are in the right position.
‒
Be careful not to lose the screws securing the tools. In case of replacement, remember these must be
class 12 K (high resistance). The use of inadequate screws can cause damage and be dangerous.

10.11.3. RAW MATERIALS UNPACKING
THIS OPERATION MUST BE CARRIED OUT BY A MAINTENANCE TECHNICIAN.
Operate as follows:
i Warning
Cutting the coil packing straps or ties is a dangerous operation also because of the possible whip lashing this
may generate.
This operation must be carried out strictly following the instructions given by the manufacturer (steelworks)
who is in any case responsible for the product sold, and it must be carried out with extreme caution.
Because of the whiplash that may be generated, this operation in any case implies high residual risks for the
people exposed, such as knocking, cutting, abrasion and crushing.
In any event, follow the suggestions below,
especially in the absence of instructions from
the manufacturer:
1
Always stand at the side and never at the front.
2
Use suitable personal protection devices (PPD).
3
Always stand with the face as far as possible
from the cutting area.
4
Use special shears with very long handles.
5
Carry out the cutting operation slowly.
GRUPPO
i Warning
‒
Because of the whiplash that may be generated, this operation in any case implies high residual risks
for the people exposed, such as knocking, cutting, abrasion and crushing:
After cutting the packing straps or ties, the coil will move suddenly due to the effect of elasticity and
this may generate dangerous situations for the people exposed.
‒
Operate in a way that the material cannot ever cause obstruction.
‒
Check carefully that the raw material is untied from straps and ties.
‒
Position the straps and the ties in an area previously shown by the employer so that they cannot cause
any obstruction.

257/403

STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
10.13.2. USING THE WINCH
The winch is an integral part of the
machine/system, to be used for the specific
declared use.
The winch is not a lifting device. It has been
designed just for the horizontal "pull" of iron
bars.
‒
Before starting to pull the rod, tension the rope
slowly until it is not perfectly taught.
‒
Only wind the rope with pull positioned in front
of the winch.
‒
If pull is lateral, rope winding is not the best,
causing wear and damage to the rope itself or
the winch.
‒
Re-wind the winch in a way that the drum coils
are well compact.
GRUPPO
•
Warning for the use of the winch
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch for uses other than those conceived.
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch to lift loads.
‒
It is forbidden to lift people, or things above people.
‒
It is forbidden to lift things vertically.
‒
Always use the winch with due care.
‒
Only use the winch for traction, according to the methods of use envisioned and described in this
instruction manual.
‒
Always check before starting any operation:
According to section Maintenance:
" Check the winch wear condition ".
‒
On replacing the worn and/or damaged parts, control that the safety devices work correctly before use.
If necessary, have it checked by After-sales Assistance Staff.
‒
Do not make modifications and do not perform welding on the winch.
These interventions can affect the integrity of the winch structure and its good working order, making
the warranty null and void.
‒
Do not use the winch in conditions that require performance higher than that for which it has been
designed; particularly regarding the entity of the pull effort. In other words, do not stress the winch
beyond its maximum pull capacity.
‒
Do not overload the winch over its maximum capacity;
See section "Technical Data":
" Technical features of the winch".
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch control forward / backward if the pulling stress exceeds the one allowed.
‒
Never attach the rope to itself in order to drag a rod, but use a suitable gripping accessory:
A -
Rebar pulling clamp.
BRANO
‒
Do not introduce parts of the body inside the clamp, to prevent crushing/entrapment.
‒
Always wear protective gloves and never run the rope through the hands during traction.
‒
Do not use extension to try and lengthen the rope.
‒
Check that the rebar is firmly attached to the grip accessory.
‒
Do not dry to release the winch drum during activation or when the winch rope is taught.
English
266/403

--- page 269 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
It is forbidden to introduce objects or parts of the body between the steel rope and the drum during the
coiling phase.
‒
It is forbidden to stop less than 1,5 m from the winch or rope during the coiling phase.
‒
It is forbidden to stop less than 1,5 m from the winch or rope when it is under tension.
‒
It is forbidden to leave the winch unattended with the steel rope stretched.
‒
Before releasing the rebar from the winch gripping accessory, completely loosen the steel rope tension:
‒
To unwind the rope from the drum, pull while holding the clamp.
‒
It is forbidden to operate the winch with less than five steel rope turns wound around the drum.
It is forbidden to completely unwind the winch rope.
‒
Keep the work area tidy and free from obstructions.
Untidiness can cause accidents.
‒
Do not knot the rope.
‒
Do not use the winch in humid environments.
Do expose the winch to rain.
‒
Do not climb over the rope or winch when the rope is taught.
‒
Do not approach the rope guide with the hands or clothing when using the winch.
‒
Do not leave tools in proximity of the winch.
‒
The winch is not designed for continuous use.
‒
Loosen the rope immediately after having terminated traction and having fixed the rod to the clamp and
the rods storage device.
10.13.3. WARNINGS AND INSTRUCTIONS
FOR THE USE OF THE REBAR PULLING
CLAMP
Assumption.
Use the (A) component for the traction of
reinforcement rebars.
The (A) component is an integral component of the
machine to be used for the specific intended use (The
(A) component should not be used for other uses).
GRUPPO
General safety precautions.
‒
The (A) component must never be used for lifting the loads.
‒
The iron bar pull operation always has a risk linked to the release of grip of the clamp on the rod to be
pulled, even if performed correctly.
Warnings for the use.
‒
The (A) component is not a lifting device.
‒
Never use the (A) component for performances beyond those for which it was designed.
‒
Never use the (A) component beyond its maximum pulling capacity.
‒
Use the (A) component exclusively for pulling, according to the methods and uses intended and
described in this manual.
‒
Do not use extension to try to lengthen the (A) component.
‒
Immediately loosen the (A) component once you have finished to pull the rebar.
‒
Loosen the cable of the (A) component completely before releasing the rebar from the clamp.
‒
Check the efficiency and integrity of the machine, in particular of the clamp, the pin and the chain
before starting the pulling operation (According to section Maintenance).
‒
Slowly tighten the (A) component until it is perfectly tensioned and then start pulling the rebar (Check
that the iron rod is attached tightly).
‒
Keep the work area tidy and free from obstructions (Untidiness can cause accidents).
‒
Do not approach the (A) component with your hands or garments when it is under tension.
‒
Do not leave tools near the component.
‒
Completely close the corresponding protection and before the pulling position of the rebar of the
component.
English
267/403

--- page 270 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
Open the protection only after having detensioned the (A) component.
‒
Always be in front of the fully closed protection, never sideways.
‒
Check that the clamp is perfectly closed before pulling the component.
‒
Do not leave the component (A) unattended when it is under tension.
‒
Do not introduce objects or parts of the body inside the chain.
‒
Do not place objects or parts of the body between the pulling unit capstan and the component (A).
‒
Do not place objects or parts of the body between the component (A) and the wire.
‒
Do not stand at a distance lower than 1,5 m from the component (A) when it is in tension.
‒
Do not attempt to unlock the rebar during the drive or when the (A) component is in tension.
‒
Do not act on the release lever of the clamp without first checking the slackening of the chain.
‒
Check and eventually replace all worn or damaged parts.
‒
Check the efficiency of the protections before operating.
‒
If there are still any doubts, please refer directly to the manufacturer's customer care.

10.13.19. USING THE WINCH
The winch is an integral part of the
machine/system, to be used for the specific
declared use.
The winch is not a lifting device. It has been
designed just for the horizontal "pull" of iron
bars.
‒
Before starting to pull the rod, tension the rope
slowly until it is not perfectly taught.
‒
Only wind the rope with pull positioned in front
of the winch.
‒
If pull is lateral, rope winding is not the best,
causing wear and damage to the rope itself or
the winch.
‒
Re-wind the winch in a way that the drum coils
are well compact.
GRUPPO
•
Warning for the use of the winch
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch for uses other than those conceived.
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch to lift loads.
‒
It is forbidden to lift people, or things above people.
‒
It is forbidden to lift things vertically.
‒
Always use the winch with due care.
‒
Only use the winch for traction, according to the methods of use envisioned and described in this
instruction manual.
‒
Always check before starting any operation:
According to section Maintenance:
" Check the winch wear condition ".
‒
On replacing the worn and/or damaged parts, control that the safety devices work correctly before use.
If necessary, have it checked by After-sales Assistance Staff.
‒
Do not make modifications and do not perform welding on the winch.
These interventions can affect the integrity of the winch structure and its good working order, making
the warranty null and void.
‒
Do not use the winch in conditions that require performance higher than that for which it has been
designed; particularly regarding the entity of the pull effort. In other words, do not stress the winch
beyond its maximum pull capacity.
‒
Do not overload the winch over its maximum capacity;
See section "Technical Data":
" Technical features of the winch".
‒
It is forbidden to use the winch control forward / backward if the pulling stress exceeds the one allowed.
‒
Never attach the rope to itself in order to drag a rod, but use a suitable gripping accessory:
English
287/403

--- page 290 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
A -
Rebar pulling clamp.
BRANO
‒
Do not introduce parts of the body inside the clamp, to prevent crushing/entrapment.
‒
Always wear protective gloves and never run the rope through the hands during traction.
‒
Do not use extension to try and lengthen the rope.
‒
Check that the rebar is firmly attached to the grip accessory.
‒
Do not dry to release the winch drum during activation or when the winch rope is taught.
‒
It is forbidden to introduce objects or parts of the body between the steel rope and the drum during the
coiling phase.
‒
It is forbidden to stop less than 1,5 m from the winch or rope during the coiling phase.
‒
It is forbidden to stop less than 1,5 m from the winch or rope when it is under tension.
‒
It is forbidden to leave the winch unattended with the steel rope stretched.
‒
Before releasing the rebar from the winch gripping accessory, completely loosen the steel rope tension:
‒
To unwind the rope from the drum, pull while holding the clamp.
‒
It is forbidden to operate the winch with less than five steel rope turns wound around the drum.
It is forbidden to completely unwind the winch rope.
‒
Keep the work area tidy and free from obstructions.
Untidiness can cause accidents.
‒
Do not knot the rope.
‒
Do not use the winch in humid environments.
Do expose the winch to rain.
‒
Do not climb over the rope or winch when the rope is taught.
‒
Do not approach the rope guide with the hands or clothing when using the winch.
‒
Do not leave tools in proximity of the winch.
‒
The winch is not designed for continuous use.
‒
Loosen the rope immediately after having terminated traction and having fixed the rod to the clamp and
the rods storage device.
10.13.20. WARNINGS AND INSTRUCTIONS
FOR THE USE OF THE REBAR PULLING
CLAMP
Assumption.
Use the (A) component for the traction of
reinforcement rebars.
The (A) component is an integral component of the
machine to be used for the specific intended use (The
(A) component should not be used for other uses).
GRUPPO
General safety precautions.
‒
The (A) component must never be used for lifting the loads.
‒
The iron bar pull operation always has a risk linked to the release of grip of the clamp on the rod to be
pulled, even if performed correctly.
Warnings for the use.
‒
The (A) component is not a lifting device.
‒
Never use the (A) component for performances beyond those for which it was designed.
‒
Never use the (A) component beyond its maximum pulling capacity.
‒
Use the (A) component exclusively for pulling, according to the methods and uses intended and
described in this manual.
‒
Do not use extension to try to lengthen the (A) component.
‒
Immediately loosen the (A) component once you have finished to pull the rebar.
‒
Loosen the cable of the (A) component completely before releasing the rebar from the clamp.
English
288/403

--- page 291 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
‒
Check the efficiency and integrity of the machine, in particular of the clamp, the pin and the chain
before starting the pulling operation (According to section Maintenance).
‒
Slowly tighten the (A) component until it is perfectly tensioned and then start pulling the rebar (Check
that the iron rod is attached tightly).
‒
Keep the work area tidy and free from obstructions (Untidiness can cause accidents).
‒
Do not approach the (A) component with your hands or garments when it is under tension.
‒
Do not leave tools near the component.
‒
Completely close the corresponding protection and before the pulling position of the rebar of the
component.
‒
Open the protection only after having detensioned the (A) component.
‒
Always be in front of the fully closed protection, never sideways.
‒
Check that the clamp is perfectly closed before pulling the component.
‒
Do not leave the component (A) unattended when it is under tension.
‒
Do not introduce objects or parts of the body inside the chain.
‒
Do not place objects or parts of the body between the pulling unit capstan and the component (A).
‒
Do not place objects or parts of the body between the component (A) and the wire.
‒
Do not stand at a distance lower than 1,5 m from the component (A) when it is in tension.
‒
Do not attempt to unlock the rebar during the drive or when the (A) component is in tension.
‒
Do not act on the release lever of the clamp without first checking the slackening of the chain.
‒
Check and eventually replace all worn or damaged parts.
‒
Check the efficiency of the protections before operating.
‒
If there are still any doubts, please refer directly to the manufacturer's customer care.

10.14.7. ADJUSTMENT OF THE PNEUMATIC
PULLING PRESSURE
On the basis of the diameter of the raw
material being processed and the number of
wires, adjust the pressure of the traction
wheels.
|
Note
‒
The traction wheel pressure must not exceed
6 bar in any case.
‒
Excessive pressure on the straightening or
traction wheel determines premature wear or
its breakage.
‒
Excessive pressure on the wheel causes
mechanical stresses that can cause damage to
the unit.
‒
The pressure must be adjusted in such a way
that it does not create the slip of the wheels on
the processing rebar.
GRUPPO
English
301/403

--- page 304 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
10.14.8. ADJUSTMENT OF THE PNEUMATIC
PRESSURE OF THE MEASURING WHEEL
Depending on the diameter of the raw material
in processing, adjust the pressure of the (A)
wheel.
|
Note
‒
The pressure value must not exceed in any
case 6 bar.
‒
An excessive pressure on the wheel results in
premature wear or breaking.
‒
Excessive pressure on the wheel causes
mechanical stresses that can cause damage to
the unit.
‒
The pressure must be adjusted in such a way
that it does not create the slip of the wheels on
the processing rebar.

excessive).

by the quality of the material.

Excessive pressure on the straightening or
traction wheel determines premature wear or
its breakage.

10.14.10. SETTING OF THE REFRIGERATOR
Set the temperature of the coolant leaving the refrigerator according to the environment temperature
and humidity.
Operate as follows:
1
Set the temperature of the coolant to the value of tmin  + 5°C (Consult the manufacturer's user manual
to set the operating parameters).
tmin Minimum temperature of the coolant.
Check the diagram tmin to determine the minimum coolant liquid temperature.
Example:
Ambient temperature= 25 °C.
Relative humidity65 %.
Minimum temperature of the coolant= 18 °C.
Notice
The temperature of the coolant below tmin causes the presence of water drops in the walls of the cooled
components.
The presence of drops of water causes problems during machine work and maintenance problems.

10.15.2. AUTOMATIC CYCLE START
i Warning
‒
Before operating the machine it is absolutely essential to have read and understood the entire manual.
‒
Make sure that there are no other people near the machine (especially children and animals) while the
machine is working or during maintenance.
Operate as follows:
1
Bring the machine in running state (Rotate the selector stop/work to work).
This operation is to be carried out by the operator in charge of loading raw materials.
2
Set the work cycle speed.
‒
Activate the control to adjust the speed of the machine work cycle.
The start of the work cycle is preceded by an
acoustic signaling.
3
Check that the device (A) works correctly after
activating the automatic cycle start control.
Operate as follows when the check result is
different than indicated.
‒
Bring the machine to "machine off" mode in
case of malfunction.
‒
Make the machine not operative until the
correct operation is restored.
GRUPPO
The start of the work cycle is preceded by a
luminous signaling.
4
Check that the device (B) works correctly after
activating the automatic cycle start control.
Operate as follows when the check result is
different than indicated:
‒
Bring the machine to "machine off" mode in
case of malfunction.
‒
Make the machine not operative until the
correct operation is restored.
GRUPPO
5
Start the work cycle.
‒
Start the work cycle in one of the following ways:
- Activate the control of automatic cycle at low speed (Slow working selector).
Keep the button pressed for 3 sec and then release it.
The machine/system will perform the work cycle set.
The machine gradually reaches the speed set by the operator.
Start the work cycle at low speed when the welding of both ends of the wire has been carried out.
- Activate the control of automatic cycle at normal speed (Normal working button).
Keep the button pressed for 3 sec and then release it.
The machine/system will perform the work cycle set.
The drive of this control allows the start of the work cycle at the speed set by the operator.
‒
If necessary, make a "stop for a production cycle" to interrupt the productive cycle, if it does not finish
by itself.
Perform one of the following commands:
- Normal stop button.
- Quick stop button.
6
When the production process has ended, operate as follows:
‒
Rotate the selector stop/work to stop.

10.15.3. AUTOMATIC CYCLE RESET
To restore the working cycle after a break it is necessary to:
1
Bring the machine/plant to "MACHINE IN FUNCTION" ; According to the section "machine/plant modes".
2
Bring the machine in running state.
The video displays the machine status and the last working cycle set.
Ensure that the cycle set is the correct cycle to be executed.
Otherwise set the correct cycle.
3
Start the work cycle (See section use, subject "Starting the automatic cycle").

How to behave:
‒
Load one coil on the new empty pay-off only when the end of the rebar has entered the rebar
processing area.
‒
Constantly supervise the unwinding and consumption of the rebar on the vertical processing platform.
‒
Always look towards the potential danger:
- Vertical platform in processing.
- Coil processing.
- Vertical pay-off.
‒
Watching constantly the elements and the rebar in motion.
‒
Stay at a safety distance from the vertical pay-off system and from the vertical platform in processing.
‒
The operator must position himself and move out of the movement area of the free end of the coil.
‒
Do not walk backwards.
‒
Exit the processing area (AL) immediately if the safety conditions to continue the operation do not
persist (Return to the operator area).
‒
Immediately release the grip from the rebar before any movement and exit the processing area(AL)
(Return to the operator area).
‒
Minimize the speed of the processing cycle before entering the processing area (AL).
Operate the control:
- Slow working selector.
‒
Constantly assess whether the operation may
be carried out in safety conditions.
BRANO
‒
Stay the time strictly necessary to complete the procedure.
‒
Make sure that the end of the coil or part of it can not be held back in its movements.
‒
Exit the processing area (AL) immediately as soon as the procedure is complete.
‒
Operate as follows if the safety conditions do not persist:
1 Exit the processing area (AL) immediately (Return to the operator area).

3 Bring the machine in "MACHINE IN MOMENTARY STOP"; According to the section " Modes of the
machine/plant".

5 Reset the automatic cycle (See section Use, topic “Reset automatic cycle”).

- Normal stop button.
2 Set the machine/plant to "MACHINE IN MOMENTARY STOP".

10.16. TRANSFER OF THE FINISHED PRODUCT TO THE EXTERNAL PRODUCTIVE UNIT
METHOD 1:
The operator responsible for transferring the finished product enters the operating area (AO) of the
machine/plant securely to transfer the finished product to the external productive unit.
Operate as follows:
1
The finished product is transferred on the external production unit by operating the manual controls of
appropriate groups, of both machine/plant and external production unit.
i Warning
‒
The external productive unit must be properly integrated and secured under the existing laws.
‒
The operator responsible for the transfer of the finished product has to be enabled to the operations
about the command and the control of the external productive unit and of the machine/plant and
trained and informed about residual risks.
GRUPPO
METHOD 2:
The finished product is transferred in an automated way without the direct intervention of the operator,
according to the appropriate setting of the external productive unit and of the machine/plant.
i Warning
‒
The external productive unit must be properly integrated and secured under the existing laws.
‒
The operator responsible for the transfer of the finished product has to be enabled to the operations
about the command and the control of the external productive unit and of the machine/plant and
trained and informed about residual risks.

During the unwinding of the wire from the pay-
off it may happen that the wire makes "knots"
which hinder the normal processing of the
machine.
The presence of a wire knot can cause damage
to the machine.
The presence of a wire knot can break the wire.
|

The unit (A) has a sensor that detects the
presence of knots and stops the machine.

(A) group completely.

11. TROUBLESHOOTING
11.1. INFORMATION FOR THE TROUBLESHOOTING
The machine/system is set up to signal automatically the main faults that can occur during its operation, by
means of alarm messages that can be seen on the operating terminal.
In some situations however, it is also possible to identify faults before the machine/system is switched on or
while it is operating.
This is why this troubleshooting guide has been prepared.
The table below shows the possible generic faults that can occur, with a corresponding list of the checks to
carry out to remove the causes.
i Warning
Carry out these operations extremely carefully with the machine/system switched off and disconnected from
power sources (electric and pneumatic).
Operate as follows:
Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE OFF state; Follow the instructions in section "Machine/system
states".
11.2. INFORMATION FOR THE TECHNICAL SUPPORT SERVICE
Please contact your local dealer or call the SCHNELL Technical Support Service directly for any faults that are
not listed in this guide, providing the information shown on the identification plate:
‒
Machine/system number.
‒
Serial number.
|
Note
Then give as much information as possible about the problem.

12. MAINTENANCE
12.1. MAINTENANCE INFORMATION
Maintenance can be mechanical, electrical, pneumatic and hydraulic.
Before operating the machine it is necessary to read and understand this manual, especially the section
“Safety instructions".
It is also necessary to comply to the safety requirements indicated in this section to safeguard the operator
and the machine by risks.
THE MAINTENANCE OPERATION OF THE MACHINE/PLANT IS ASSIGNED TO THE MAINTENANCE
OPERATOR.
Notice
‒
Scheduled maintenance involves a series of operations, adjustments and parts replacement to be done
at regular intervals, the aim of which is to ensure that the machine/system functions efficiently. The
purpose is also to avoid some parts breaking, that, if they do break, may cause even more serious
damage to others.
‒
The scheduled maintenance frequency is the minimum advisable and this should be increased when the
machine/system is subject to heavy use.
‒
Ordering and using only original spare parts to make the machine/system more reliable and safe.
‒
Perform maintenance of the accessories according to the specific user manual.
12.2. TRANSFER OF THE FINISHED PRODUCT FOR MAINTENANCE
The operation is referred to the operator.
Operate as follows before starting maintenance operations:
Operate in the most appropriate way to free the machine/plant from the finished handwork/product in safety.
See section Use, subject:
“Transfer of the finished product to the external productive unit”.
12.3. HANDWORK UNLOADING FOR MAINTENANCE
Operate as follows before starting maintenance operations:
Operate in the most appropriate way to free the machine/plant from the finished handwork/product in safety.
12.4. RAW MATERIAL UNLOADING FOR MAINTENANCE
Operate as follows before starting maintenance operations:
Operate the raw material unloading from the machine in the most appropriate way.

12.5. BLOCK THE ENERGY SOURCES OF THE EXTERNAL PRODUCTIVE UNIT
i Warning
Make sure that the energy sources of the external productive unit inside the machine and the related
disconnectors have been blocked to prevent the unexpected restart.
Operate as follows:
1
Make sure that the energy sources of the external productive unit inside the machine and the related
disconnectors have been blocked to prevent the unexpected restart.
2
Block the energy sources of the external productive unit in the appropriate manner.
12.6. MAINTENANCE SAFETY RULES
The maintenance operations may only be carried out by authorised personnel suitably trained and
skilled and expressly authorised by the company using the machine/system. The system maintenance
interventions must also be carried out respecting the “safety requirements for use of the
machine/system” described in these “Instructions for use”.
"In addition to what already said, please follow the instructions listed below":
‒
Consult the technical documentation provided by the suppliers of individual machine/system parts,
before carrying out maintenance operations.
‒
In particular consult:
Instructions for use” and the diagrams of the electrical, hydraulic, pneumatic systems, etc.
‒
In any event, only operate on the machines if you have the appropriate technical know-how.
Notice
The manufacturer of any parts supplied is always available for any further clarification of maintenance
procedures.
i Warning
Disconnect the machine/system from any power sources before starting any maintenance, parts replacement,
repairs, cleaning, lubricating, adjustment etc.
Always take this precaution, even if not expressly specified.
Operate as follows:
1
Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE
OFF state; Follow the instructions in section
"Machine/system states".
2
Display a "Maintenance in progress" sign
(According to section Maintenance).
GRUPPO
i Warning
‒
Check that there is no residual accumulated power, after disconnecting the machine/system from power
sources.
‒
If some residual power has accumulated, this should be discharged before carrying out any operations
on the machine/system, since this power could be a source of danger.
‒
Locate the machine/system in a manner that makes it possible to carry out all the necessary
maintenance operations, using the appropriate tools and equipment (ladders, trestles, vacuum cleaners
etc.) in the maximum safety conditions.
‒
Always check, before carrying out operations and using appropriate tools, that there is no electric power
to the machine/system.

i Warning
There are electrostatic load batteries (capacitors, etc.)
inside the electrical panel.
Wait 15 minutes for these to discharge before working
on the control panel.
GRUPPO
The main switches on the electric control panels and the main compressed air cocks are normally lockable in
the off position, each with its own key, in order to avoid accidental start-up.
Use the special keys provided to lock the controls while maintenance is being performed, to avoid the hazard
of power sources being accidentally switched on by others.
To this end, and to ensure that no one has any copies of these keys, they must be kept personally by the
machine/system operator.
If the machine consists of individual parts which may be dismantled, disassemble them and handle and
transport each part (See section “Transport”).
The electrical cabinet may remain open for as long as the maintenance and tooling technician works on the
electrical panel.
Before moving away from the electrical panel/cabinet:
Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE OFF state; Follow the instructions in section "Machine/system
states".

i Warning
‒
Do not use water or liquids with unknown properties, in case of fire.
‒
Use only the correct extinguishers for the type of fire ongoing.
i Warning
‒
Wear protective goggles when using compressed or suctioned air.
‒
Never point the jet of air at people, particularly not at their face.
Notice
‒
Don’t blow dust into the machine/system when using compressed air.
‒
Use indicated gloves when using oily or thinner substances.
‒
Clean and dry carefully the surfaces that have not to be greased that might be stained by these
substances, in particular floors.
When changing or disposing of lubricants and chemical products, follow the instructions in the safety
information sheets for the products used.
Scraps and calamine dust should be collected and disposed correctly with respect to local laws.
Do not dump lubricants (or chemical products in general) in the environment; instead, collect them and
dispose of them correctly with respect to the local laws.
Reassemble and correctly tighten all the dismantled parts (taking particular care with fixed and
moveable covers and safety components), before starting the machine/system up again.
i Warning
If the machine/system breaks down or malfunctions, especially if the fault involves the safety components,
stop the machine/system immediately and disconnect it from power sources.
Then remove all the copies of the keys that enable it to be used, until the fault is completely rectified.
It is the operator’s responsibility to maintain the machine/system and all its components, particularly its safety
mechanisms and devices, in full working order.

12.9. MOVING UNITS FOR MAINTENANCE
Notice
According to the configuration of the machine/plant some units hindering the correct maintenance activity
might be disconnected or moved.
Once completed the maintenance activity, operate to restore the machine/plant in its operative and safety
functions.
i Warning
Operate in the most appropriate and safest way to make the working area intended for the maintenance
accessible (See section Installation).
12.10.LOSS OF LUBRICANT
If oil leaks are noticed, before eliminating the marks, proceed as follows:
1
Set the machine/plant in “OFF” mode.
2
Take all necessary precautions to avoid slipping hazards and use suitable personal protective devices for
chemical lubricant substances.
3
Check the machine carefully to find out the exact point/points of the oil leaking (identify these locations
with a marker pen).
4
Carefully clean up the oil leak from both the machine and the floor.
5
Make sure the machine cannot be used until fully reset.
12.11.LOSS OF COMPRESSED AIR
i Warning
Wear appropriate protection devices (PPE).
Do the following if you notice compressed air spills:
1
Set the machine/plant in “OFF” mode (Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE OFF state; Follow
the instructions in section "Machine/system states").
2
Carefully observe the machine to find the exact point where the leak occurs (mark this spot with a
pencil).
3
Make sure the machine cannot be used until fully reset.
12.12.LEAK OF COOLANT
i Warning
Danger of slipping.
Operate as follows when liquid spills are noticed:
1
Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE OFF state; Follow the instructions in section
"Machine/system states".
2
Take the necessary precautions to avoid the danger of slipping and to use suitable personal protective
devices for liquid chemicals.
3
Carefully observe the machine to find the exact point where the leak occurs (mark this spot with a
pencil).
4
Clean the plane surface.
5
Make sure the machine cannot be used until fully reset.

12.13.WARNING FOR THE USE OF SPARE PARTS
The use of non-original spare parts may result in malfunctioning of the machine, leading to risks for the
operator and people working near the machine.
|
Note
Always use exclusively genuine original replacement parts supplied by the Manufacturer.
12.14.WARNING FOR THE WORKS IN HEIGHT
i Warning
The means used to take the operator to the working height must comply with the instructions of the safety
regulation on workplaces as regards to “temporary works at heights”.
The mobile ladder (A) with stand or the access platforms (B) have the minimum useful features, to reach the
safety work height.
The ladders are considered mobile, when they can be moved where necessary.
GRUPPO
In order to comply with conformity criteria of the applicable regulation, the mobile ladders must be:
‒
Built according to the standard UNI EN 131 part one and two.
‒
Accompanied by a short description, featuring constituent elements as well as instructions for a correct
use for preservation and maintenance.
‒
Mark with the symbol 'EN 131' along with the name of the manufacturer, the type of ladder, year and
month of production, maximum permissible load, angle of inclination.
‒
Accompanied by conformity statements with technical standard.
‒
Ladders with a height, such as to stick out of the access surface by at least one metre.
‒
Closing system of the standing platform for the protection of the operator.
Notice
The CE marking placed on the ladder is not a guarantee of conformity with the standard UNI EN 131.
English
334/403

--- page 337 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
The precautions to comply with for the use of the ladder are the following:
‒
Closed shoes with non-slip sole, in order to avoid stumbling and slipping on the ladder steps.
‒
Always make sure the ladder is steadily fixed to the ground, before climbing on it.
‒
When climbing and getting off, always proceed with the face towards the ladder, making sure to
correctly place the foot on the step, especially when getting off.
‒
Keep your own centre of gravity as inside as possible to the standing platform, without unbalancing
outwards, in order to avoid the overturning of the ladder.
‒
Fasten with hooks or other systems the tools being used to impede their accidental fall.
‒
Be careful when tools are used or when works, producing horizontal thrusts, which may have the ladder
slide on the floor, are carried out.
In order to counterbalance the thrusts, it is recommended to be supported by another person or perform
a fastening to a steady wall, which ensures a suitable anchoring.
‒
Do not place the ladder on irregular floors or near steep gradients.
The use of lifting means, different from the mobile ladder (fixed or mobile scaffolding, man baskets, car lifts,
etc.) entails the adoptions of other safety precautions.
The operator, performing maintenance
operations at heights (temporary works at
heights) must wear a “Safety belt with wire
rope” with the following features:
‒
It is necessary to use the safety belts with
straps and legs harness along with a suitable
wire rope, limiting the fall no more than 1,5 m
and ending with a safety carbine hook.
GRUPPO
‒
The use of the wire rope must occur along with energy absorbing devices (heat sinks), because even
falls from a medium height may cause high stopping power.
‒
The wire rope must be fastened, either directly or via a sliding ring along a suitably stretched rope, to
stable parts of the fixed or temporary works.
‒
The rope and all the constituent elements of the belt must have sections, so as to resist to stresses,
deriving from any fall of the worker.
‒
The length of the wire rope must be such as to limit the fall to no more than 1,50 m.
i Warning
Check the efficiency conditions of the protective device (PPE) before wearing it and that it bears the CE
marking on all its constituent elements.
|
Note
Receive the CE statement of conformity of the belts.
English
335/403

12.22.CLEANLINESS
The following are the cleanliness maintenance operations for the machine / plant and its periodicity.
English
337/403

--- page 340 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
12.22.1. WARNINGS FOR THE CLEANING
Refer to safety prescriptions specific of this section for safeguarding the security of the operator and of the
machine/system.
In addition to improving its functioning, cleaning the machine/system facilitates maintenance, inspection and
repairs.
Keep handles and decking free of oil, grease and dirt to minimise the risk of slipping and falling.
i Warning
‒
Do not use water or other products that
conduct electricity.
‒
Do not use chemical solvents for cleaning.
At most use diesel oil, though this should never
be sprayed, poured or brushed on.
Use a slightly dampened cloth for cleaning.
Notice
Do not use an air gun:
The lubricated parts may have a build-up of
scale, dust or other impurities that can damage
the greased part.
GRUPPO
Scraps and calamine dust should be collected and
disposed correctly with respect to local laws.
BRANO
Reassemble and correctly tighten all the dismantled parts (taking particular care with fixed and moveable
covers and safety components), before starting the machine/system up again.
12.22.2. CLEANING THE WALKWAYS (EVERY8 WORKING HOURS OR AT EVERY SHIFT END)
Clean the walking surface over the entire machine/system area.
The machine/system area must be free from any obstructions.
12.22.3. GENERAL CLEANING (EVERY 40 OPERATING HOURS)
Using an industrial vacuum cleaner, first clean the inner parts (front and back) and then the outer parts of the
machine/system.
Provide the electrical components and connections properly.
Notice
‒
Be careful not to knock the electrical components.
‒
An air gun must never be used.
Carefully clean the machine tools (pins, sliding tracks, etc.).
English
338/403

--- page 341 ---
STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
To avoid premature wear of the gaskets remove dust, impurities and dirt from the pneumatic cylinders stems,
using non-abrasive materials or detergents.
Notice
‒
This procedure must be performed with care to avoid scratching or scoring the rods.
‒
Clean the painted metalwork parts with non-corrosive detergent.
12.22.4. CLEANING THE DATAPLATES AND INDICATOR LIGHTS (EVERY160 OPERATING
HOURS)

plates, especially the safety pictograms.
All the messages on plates and labels attached to the machine/system or its parts must be clearly legible.
If any of these have deteriorated, replacements must be ordered directly from the manufacturer.

12.23.2. INFORMATION FOR GREASING THE
PUMP
|
Note
Use grease ISO UNI 3498 - xbcea 1 /
ISO UNI 3498 - xbcea 2 or equivalent (See
the comparison table further on in the manual).
Operate as follows:
1
Carefully clean the lubrication point.
2
Lubricating using the grease nipples.
Choose the lubricant according to the
environment temperature.
Pump the pump slowly.
For each complete squeeze of the grease pump
provided, about 2 grams of grease are injected.
3
Fill all the greasing nipples through the greasing
pump by injecting two squeezes of grease for
every intervention.
Always clean the heads of the grease nipples, in
order to avoid dirt infiltrations.
|
Note
Centralized lubrication points, where a certain
number of greasing units are gathered and
single greasing units are signalled by proper
labels.
Notice
Damaged or clogged lubricators must always
be replaced.
GRUPPO

while in fact it could be below minimum.

12.24.MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE
The following are the mechanical maintenance operations for the machine / plant and its periodicity.
12.24.1. WARNINGS FOR THE MECHANICAL MAINTENANCE
Refer to safety prescriptions specific of this section for safeguarding the security of the operator and of the
machine/system.
i Warning
‒
The frequency of the intervention gaps are the minimum advised and they depend on the onerousness
of use of the machine/system.
‒
Disconnect the machine/system from any power sources before starting any maintenance, parts
replacement, repairs, cleaning, lubricating, adjustment etc.
Always take this precaution, even if not expressly specified.

STRETCH DRIVE 6-16/COLD2 MIRROR
12.24.16. SHARP BLADE CHECK (EVERY 8 OPERATING HOURS)
The cutting blades are reversible and can hence be used several times by turning them over.
Check wear on the blade. If it is worn, replace it.
Notice
The blade is to be considered worn when a burred cut is obtained and/or the blade itself appears deformed on
the cutting edge.
Working with a blade with a ruined cutting edge significantly increases the force applied to the fixed blade.
This does not cause it to break immediately but it surely shortens its life, and invites breakage through fatigue.
Therefore, this type of problem cannot be covered by any type of guarantee.
Working with a blade with a deformed cutting edge stresses the cutting unit abnormally with possible
consequence of component breakage.
Replace the blade at least every 1000 work hours whatever its condition.
12.24.17. REPLACING THE BLADE (EVERY
1000 OPERATING HOURS)
Operate as follows:
1
Loosen and remove the component retaining
screw (A).
2
Remove the knife (B).
3
Clean the housing and lubricate the walls.
4
Turn the blade to use it again for the second
time or replace it by a new one.
5
Fit the (A) component.
Insert and tighten the fixing screws.
Notice
Check that the lock screws just removed is
intact, if it is not, replace them exclusively with
identical class 12 K screws (high resistance).
Screw the lock screw back into place.
i Warning
The use of inadequate screws can cause
damage.

12.24.20. CHECK THE WEAR AND THE TENSION OF THE TRANSMISSION CHAIN (EVERY
1000 OPERATING HOURS)
Follow the instructions below:
‒
Check the status of wear of the component.
This type of control must verify that chain structure is not modified or deformed from its initial state by
cracks or snags, for example wear on the side of the chains and the related pins can result from
abnormal fretting and is a sign that the chain must be replaced, verifying what caused the malfunction.
In case of excess wear, these must be replaced.
‒
Make, if necessary, the adjustment of the
transmission chain to restore the right tension.
Keeping the chain rings correctly tensioned
prevents excessive wear of the chain itself and
of the gear wheel connected to it.

12.25.1. WARNING FOR THE ELECTRICAL MAINTENANCE
Refer to safety prescriptions specific of this section for safeguarding the security of the operator and of the
machine/system.
Only a maintenance technician is allowed to carry out maintenance/troubleshooting/repairs and open the
electrical panel.
The maintenance technician must be adequately trained and qualified.
i Warning
Before touching the part involved, always check that there is no power by measuring the voltage between the
concerned parts and between the parts and the earthing system, using an electrical tester.
i Warning
There are electrostatic batteries (capacitors etc.)
inside the electric panel, wait 15 minutes to allow
them to discharge.
GRUPPO
i Warning
The electrical components associated with the SAFETY features of the machine/system must be checked at
every shift.
If a fault occurs, the machine/system immediately goes into “Emergency stop” mode, until the cause of the
fault that triggered the emergency is eliminated.
|
Note
If a fault occurs, all the limit switches are signalled acoustically and visually by the control console computer.
Check the fault shown on the monitor and replace the limit switch if necessary.
Should electric malfunctionings occur, the first thing to check are the fuses and the thermal cut-outs.
Burned fuses must be replaced with models with identical characteristics.
i Warning
Replacement fuses of a different type from the original are an obvious cause of damage to the
machine/system and present a serious hazard to people.
If the thermal cut-outs have tripped, reset them by pushing the black button marked with the number one.
i Warning
Fuses may blow and therml circuit breakers may trip due to temporary or pre-existing problems. If they
continue to blow/trip once they have been replaced or reset, stop the machine/system immediately and
proceed as follows:
1
Bring the machine/system into the MACHINE
OFF state; Follow the instructions in section
"Machine/system states".
2
Display a "Maintenance in progress" sign
(According to section Maintenance).
3
Contact the nearest service centre of the
manufacturer.
GRUPPO
English
393/403

--- page 396 ---

Only a maintenance/tooling technician is allowed to carry out maintenance/troubleshooting/repairs and open

12.26.1. WARNINGS FOR THE MAINTENANCE OF COMPRESSED AIR SYSTEM
Refer to safety prescriptions specific of this section for safeguarding the security of the operator and of the
machine/system.
i Warning
Carry out the operations with the circuit completely depressurised.
If possible, directly switch off the compressor that feeds the machine/system.
i Warning
‒
Valves with closed centres are fitted in the machine/system (For further details see the pneumatic
diagram).
‒
Pneumatic pressure is present in the tubes.
‒
The air contained in the tubes can maintain the mechanical device in suspension or loaded.
i Warning
Residual risks:
Crushing, cutting, shearing, abrasion, dragging, flying objects, whiplashing, etc.
Operate as follows to discharge the residual
compressed air present in a closed centre
valve:
1
Turn the screw(A), from the position "0" to the
position "1", in order to discharge the residual
air present in the pneumatic system.
2
Turn the screw(A) from the position "1" to the
position "0".
3
Turn the screw(B), from the position "0" to the
position "1", in order to discharge the residual
air present in the pneumatic system.
4
Turn the screw(B) from the position "1" to the
position "0".

seals.
‒

‒
Check that the seals, couplings and plug-in electric connections are firmly positioned so that no
detergent enters.
‒
Wash with warm water and soap.

13. SCRAPPING AND DISPOSAL
13.1. GENERAL INFORMATION FOR THE DISPOSAL
|
Note
The manufacturer’s machines do not require any special precautions at the time of disposal because more
than 90% (by weight) of the machines is made of recyclable materials.
13.2. OPERATIONS FOR THE DEMOLITION AND THE DISPOSAL
The machine/system must be dismantled taking safety precautions that take account of the logistical
and environmental situation and of the age and condition of the machine/system itself.
Nonetheless, follow the general rules below:
1
Wear protective clothing and accessories (helmet, safety footwear, gloves, and safety spectacles and
facemask if necessary) approved in accordance with statutory safety legislation.
2
Disconnect the machine from all power sources.
3
Check all pressurised systems, depressurising them if necessary.
4
Drain and collect the remaining oil and dispose of it in accordance with current local laws.
5
Make the machine/system inoperative and unusable by breaking all of its major operating components
and move it to a location that ensures no one is able to access it.
6
Use suitable lifting devices as indicated in the chapter transport and placement.
7
Break the latches on the doors of the machine and of the enclosures where people or animals could get
trapped.
8
Disassemble the machine/system into small, easily transportable units.
9
For disposal of the machine/system, separate out the non-polluting and polluting materials (oils,
electrical batteries, insulators, plastic and rubber materials etc.).
10
Never set fire to the machine or part of it, since the products of combustion of plastic materials and
paints may develop noxious and polluting gases.
Concerning the laws on “safety in the work environment” take all the instructions in this manual into
consideration and, in particular, all the paragraphs marked with the symbol:
i Danger
i Warning
I Caution
i Warning
The machine should ideally be scrapped and disposed of by specialised and qualified personnel who are in
possession of specific information and equipment for this type of operation.
In order for us to continue to improve on this product, please let us know if there are any errors
or omissions in the manuals provided, particularly if these concern safety, or let us have your
suggestions for improving the machine/system or our support services, or anything else you
would like to tell us.
Thank you for your cooperation we wish you:
Good job!
English
403/403
